## "In My Own Words" — Free Composition + Virtual Keyboard (June 2026) — decided, not yet built

Free composition is one of the **invariants** (Configuration Model): the user must always have a path to type/compose something the AI did not generate and have the app speak it in the selected voice. The UI's **composer-access region** (UI-Design.docx four-region layout) is where this lives. "In my own words" is the user-facing name for that path.

**Why this surfaced now:** to test the worldview Tier model (and any response shaping) by hand before the AI generation path exercises it, we need a manual way to compose an utterance and speak it. So the simple composer is no longer just a far-future nicety — it's the **test harness for worldview output** and is wanted alongside Build Step 1.

### Settled decisions

- **The keyboard is the easy part; voice + return-to-UI are essentially free.** Any keyboard (OS or in-app) just deposits text into a focused field in the page. The app then speaks it via the existing `tts.speak()` path (already uses the app-selected voice), and the app UI was never actually left. No integration risk on the "speak in the app's voice" / "return to the app" requirements regardless of keyboard choice.

- **OS / browser keyboard cannot be reliably summoned, and is a black box — so it is a *fallback*, not the foundation.** Technical reality on a Windows tablet (Edge/Chrome):
  - There is **no web API to programmatically launch the Windows touch keyboard.** It auto-appears only when an editable element (`input`/`textarea`/`contenteditable`) receives focus *and* the device is in a touch context. "Invoke from a button" therefore means "focus a text field and hope the OS pops the keyboard."
  - On a Surface with the type cover attached (laptop mode) it often won't auto-pop at all. Behavior varies by tablet mode, Windows version, and browser.
  - The Chromium **VirtualKeyboard API** (`navigator.virtualKeyboard.show()`) only manages an already-available keyboard's overlay; it still requires focus on an editable element. Not a reliable on-demand "open system keyboard" switch.
  - It gives **zero control** over layout, key size, spacing, prediction, or access method — exactly the things that matter for a CP user's motor accessibility.

- **The real path is an in-app virtual keyboard.** It is the only option that:
  1. **Honors access-method independence** (engine emits a palette descriptor; renderers present it — direct select now, scanning / eye-gaze later). An OS keyboard can't participate in the future scanning renderer; an in-app keyboard *is* just another renderer.
  2. **Unlocks AI-assisted composition** — type a few words → expand into a full sentence *in the user's voice* (worldview model); personalized word/phrase prediction. The OS keyboard can touch none of this.
  3. Keeps visual + behavioral consistency (key sizing, triple-coding, dark mode, latency dots).

### Build order (decided)

1. **Type-and-speak composer — DONE (June 14 2026).** Added an "In your own words:" region to `index.html` (textarea + Speak + Clear), `ui.js` helpers (`onSpeakClick`/`onClearComposerClick`/`getComposerText`/`clearComposer`), and `handleSpeakComposed()` in `app.js` → `tts.speak()` in the selected voice. MVP behavior: speaks the composed text, guards empty/whitespace input, returns status to Listening/Ready. Works with whatever keyboard the OS offers (and a hardware keyboard in dev). This is the MVP realization of the free-composition invariant. A "use system keyboard" affordance was not added (and must never be depended on for summoning the OS keyboard). **Deferred sub-decision — RESOLVED (Ken, Pass 14, June 21 2026), v0.3.19: tapping Speak IS taking the floor, so it behaves like selecting a response.** Previously the composer only spoke the text and left the partner's turn open, recording still running. Now `handleSpeakComposed()` mirrors `handleMoveSelected`: it stops the filler ladder, invalidates in-flight generation (`generationToken++`), **stops recording** (`stt.stopListening()`), captures + clears the partner's `currentPartnerText`, speaks, **terminates the partner's statement** (`engine.selectMove({text})` pops the partner FPP → floor OPEN, records `lastUserUtterance` so Say-again works on it), clears the stale palette + composer, **commits the exchange to history** (`commitExchange(raw, text, -1)` — partner turn cleaned + logged, then the composed user turn), and **resumes listening iff auto-resume is armed** via the shared `resumeOrIdle()` (same `manualListenArmed && loadAutoRelisten()` gate as a palette pick — so it only re-listens when the user has manually started at least once this session). `commitExchange` now logs `allOptions: []` for index < 0 (free-composed / repair utterances aren't palette picks, so no stale palette is attributed to them). Verified in preview: `selectMove` pops a seeded partner INVITATION FPP (floor self→open, stack emptied); clicking Speak clears the composer + `#responseOptions`, runs the idle branch of `resumeOrIdle` (listening not armed), no console errors. *Mic-stop and the armed-resume branch need the tablet mic to confirm live.*
**"Reframe" — a second verb on the composer (proposed Ken, June 21 2026; one-shot version BUILT v0.3.20).** In addition to **Speak** (say the box text verbatim), a **Reframe** action hands the box text to the AI as *steering/context* and regenerates the suggested responses around it — "give me new options taking this into account." The box is dual-purpose: same input, two verbs.
   - **BUILT (v0.3.20):** `index.html` adds `#reframeBtn` between Speak and Clear (placeholder updated to name both verbs); `ui.onReframeClick`; `keyboard.js` `KEEP_OPEN_CONTROLS` includes `#reframeBtn` (so the on-screen keyboard doesn't hide when it's tapped — same fix as Speak/Clear). `llm.generateResponses` takes an optional `opts.steer` string → a `steerBlock` appended to the system prompt that frames the text as "additional context AND direction… being user-authored, they are TRUE and override the 'keep it general' caution" (so it is exactly the channel for supplying the specifics the v0.3.18 anti-fabrication rule forbids the model from inventing). `app.js` `handleReframe()` mirrors `handleRegenerate`: guards to an active partner turn with a palette (`if (!currentPartnerText || !lastPalette.length)`), re-runs generation with `{ steer }`, refreshes ONLY the palette via `engine.refreshPalette` (stack/mode/floor untouched — no duplicate FPP), and **clears the box on success** (kept on error for retry). Verified in preview: button renders (v0.3.20); the guard fires with no API call and keeps the box; a stubbed call confirms the steer text + guidance framing + user-authored-TRUE override reach the system prompt and the result parses. *Full success path with a real captured turn + live API needs the tablet.*
   - **Why it's strong / where it fits:** it's the channel by which the user *injects ground truth the AI lacks*, so it's the natural complement to the v0.3.18 anti-fabrication guardrail — type "I actually beat Tyler at chess last night" → Reframe → the AI can now build options around a *true* event instead of being held to the general. (A lightweight, in-the-moment version of the future episodic-events idea, thought #2a.) The text may carry content ("I'm worried about the cost") and/or direction ("keep it short, lean toward declining"); frame the box to the model as "the user's guidance for how to respond / what to convey" — no need to make the user pick a mode.
   - **Mechanism — ~80% already built (it's a *guided* regenerate).** Reuse the v0.3.16 "Show me different options" seam: re-run `llm.generateResponses` for the SAME partner turn with an optional `steer` string (alongside the existing `avoid`), then refresh ONLY the palette via `engine.refreshPalette` (stack / mode / floor untouched — no duplicate FPP). Regenerate *is* "Reframe with empty guidance," so the two can share one code path. Touches `llm.js` (a `steer` opt in the prompt), `app.js` (a `handleReframe` modeled on `handleRegenerate`), `ui.js` + `index.html` (the Reframe button).
   - **DECIDED — one-shot, not sticky (Ken, June 21 2026).** The steer applies to *this* regeneration only, then is discarded — it is NOT carried into subsequent turns. **Clear the box after Reframe** (Ken's call): both Speak and Reframe *consume* the box, so an empty box reliably means "nothing pending." Keeping the text would falsely signal that the steer is still in effect (sticky). (Unlike the earlier thought of keeping it to iterate — consistency of "empty = no pending action" wins.)
   - **The sticky/persistent version is the conversation-goal control — deferred to the Goals subsystem.** A steer that *persists* across turns is literally the **conversation goal** layer of the three-layer Goals model (see *Conversation & Relationship Goals*). That version needs a way to *see* and *clear* the standing steer and rules for layering with relationship/disposition goals — build it there, not here. **[→ folds into the Conversation & Relationship Goals build as "Reframe = the mid-conversation conversation-goal control."]**
   - **Phase:** the one-shot Reframe is **Phase 1, near-term** — no situational awareness or RAG needed, slots onto the regenerate plumbing, patch-level. **Smaller things to pin at build time:** button label ("Reframe" is Ken's word; "Steer options" an alt — must be unmistakably distinct from Speak for the target user); the no-partner-turn case (first cut can guard like regenerate's `if (!currentPartnerText || !lastPalette.length) return`, or later shape openers in INITIATING).
   - **Academic grounding (Ken, July 13 2026):** `Reframe-Concepts-for-AAC-Architecture.docx` synthesizes the academic frameworks behind "common ways a user might want to redirect a conversation" — Goffman's frame analysis/footing (the literal origin of the word "reframe"), Halliday's SFL register theory (field/tenor/mode), Martin & White's Appraisal Theory (attitude/engagement/graduation), and Grice + Brown & Levinson's pragmatics/politeness theory — and turns them into a 7-category taxonomy of redirect types (topic pivot, tone/register, stance/assertiveness, length/pacing, ground-truth injection, emotional coloring, footing/alignment as the umbrella). Notes that two already-shipped design choices are, in hindsight, direct implementations of these frameworks: the anti-fabrication rule (v0.3.18) = Grice's Quality maxim; the DISPREFERRED slot's hedge+account rule = Brown & Levinson negative politeness. Recommends any future preset/quick-select layer for Reframe (see the sticky-steer note above) be organized by these independent axes rather than as one flat list. Not built — a reference doc, same status as `CA-Concepts-for-AAC-Architecture.docx` for the rest of the engine.

2. **In-app virtual keyboard (later).** A real renderer: on-screen keys sized for direct select, integrated with the app's look and the future access-method renderers.

   **Keyboard-selection design — BUILT & verified (June 14 2026); awaiting Ken's usability review on the test tablet.** Shipped: `app/js/keyboard.js` (the shared on-screen keyboard — digit row + QWERTY + shift/caps, space, backspace, Enter, and `, . ' -`; keys act on `pointerdown` + `preventDefault` so the target field keeps focus and the caret never moves; inserts at the caret and dispatches `input`; Enter saves single-line fields / inserts newline in the composer textarea). Settings → General radio "Keyboard for typing" (`index.html`), wired in `app.js` `openSettings`/`saveSettings`; `storage.loadKeyboardMode`/`saveKeyboardMode` (default `physical`); CSS for the panel + radios; `keyboard.init()` + `setMode(loadKeyboardMode())` at app init; sw precache + CACHE_VERSION → `aac-v0.2.6`. A `MutationObserver` on `#worldviewContent` sets `inputmode="none"` on dynamically-built `.wv-text` cards so the Windows keyboard is suppressed before first focus. Verified in preview (synthetic `focusin`, since the harness window isn't system-focused): onscreen mode shows the keyboard + suppresses the OS keyboard and types into both the composer and worldview fields; physical mode never shows it and leaves `inputmode` empty; Settings radio round-trips; no console errors. *Build details below remain the authoritative description.*

   **Keyboard-selection design — original spec (Ken, June 14 2026).** Motivating problem: on a Surface, Windows shows *no* on-screen keyboard when the type cover is attached in laptop position, but auto-pops its own keyboard when the cover is folded back or detached. We want the app's keyboard in that second case. **Detection finding:** the browser exposes *no* reliable signal for physical-keyboard attachment or Surface type-cover posture — `navigator.devicePosture` only reports foldable-*screen* state (`folded`/`continuous`), the Keyboard API reports layout not attachment, and there is no `isKeyboardAttached`. The only *effective-posture* inferences are reactive (watch `visualViewport`/VirtualKeyboard `geometrychange` to see whether Windows pops its keyboard on focus) or one-way (a trusted hardware `keydown` proves a keyboard exists; silence proves nothing). **Ken's decision: do NOT auto-detect or fall back. A user-set Settings parameter decides, and it drives behavior until the user explicitly changes it** ("won't be an issue in practice"). Spec:
   - **Setting:** Settings → General, two radio buttons "Keyboard for typing": ① *My physical keyboard* (default) ② *On-screen keyboard (app's own)*. Stored in `aac_settings` as `keyboardMode: 'physical' | 'onscreen'` via new `storage.js` `loadKeyboardMode()`/`saveKeyboardMode()` (default `'physical'`).
   - **Behavior:** `physical` → app renders no keyboard, inputs keep normal `inputmode` (if the OS pops its keyboard when folded, that's the OS; the user owns that choice). `onscreen` → in-scope text fields get `inputmode="none"` (the reliable Edge/Chrome switch that suppresses the Windows keyboard) and focusing one shows the app's own virtual keyboard. Read at field-focus time so a Settings change takes effect on the next field without reload.
   - **Scope:** the "In your own words" composer + the "About Me" questionnaire inputs, built as one shared keyboard component. Settings' own fields (API key) stay on OS/physical for now (Setup-tier, rare, often supporter-entered).
   - **Dependency / phasing:** the parameter is the easy ~10%; the virtual-keyboard *renderer* is the bulk and is this build-order item #2. The two MUST ship together — a setting that suppresses the OS keyboard and then shows nothing is a regression. Build = (a) `storage.js` accessors, (b) Settings radio group + wiring + CACHE_VERSION bump, (c) the shared app keyboard component (accessible key sizing for CP motor needs, inserts into focused field, Speak/Enter consistent with composer, dark mode, future scanning-renderer compatible), (d) composer + worldview focus handlers that summon/dismiss it. *Not yet started — awaiting Ken's go-ahead to build.*
3. **AI-assisted composition (later, after worldview lands).** Few-words → full-sentence expansion in the user's voice; personalized prediction.

Open sub-questions deferred to build time: where the composer sits relative to the move palette (screen allocation — already an open UI question), and whether the type-and-speak composer should also push the spoken utterance into conversation history like a selected response.

## Auto-update on launch / Start (June 14 2026), v0.2.10

**Problem (Ken):** the app didn't pick up new deployments on launch or when Start was pressed — the old "Ctrl+Shift+R dance." Two causes: (a) the network-first worker's `fetch(request)` still hit the **browser HTTP cache** (GitHub Pages serves `Cache-Control: max-age=600`), so a launch within 10 min of a deploy got stale assets; (b) the registration never rolled a redeployed worker into the running page — a new `sw.js` installed but the page kept the version it launched with.

**Fix (three parts):**
1. **`sw.js` revalidates.** Runtime fetch is `fetch(new Request(request, { cache: 'no-cache' }))` (ETag revalidation — can't serve stale within the max-age window). Precache `addAll` uses `new Request(u, { cache: 'reload' })` so the precached shell is fresh too. (`self.skipWaiting()` on install + `clients.claim()` on activate were already there.)
2. **Auto-reload on new worker (`index.html`).** A `controllerchange` listener reloads the page once when a freshly-activated worker takes control. Guarded with `hadController` (skip the first-ever install, which has no prior controller) and a `reloading` flag (no loop). `reg.update()` is called on `load` to check for a new worker every launch.
3. **Update check on Start (`app.js` `handleStart`).** `navigator.serviceWorker.getRegistration().then(r => r && r.update())` — a cheap no-op when nothing's new; if a new worker is found it activates and the `controllerchange` handler reloads. Covers the "left open, redeploy, press Start" case.

APP_VERSION / CACHE_VERSION → `0.2.10`. **Verified in preview:** worker registers + activates + controls the page, version 0.2.10 in About, no console errors, **no reload loop** on localhost (controllerchange doesn't fire after the first claim when nothing's new). *The redeploy→auto-reload path itself can't be exercised in the preview (needs a real new deployment) — confirm on the test tablet that a fresh deploy now appears on relaunch without a hard refresh.*

## "What's new" post-update notice (July 4 2026), v0.5.62

The companion to the auto-update above (Ken — modeled on the Keyguard Designer web app's system): because the app silently reloads itself into a new version, the user never sees *what* changed. After an auto-update, the freshly-loaded build now shows a short, plain-language "What's new" summary of everything that changed since the version they last saw.

**Architecture (four parts, mirroring Keyguard):**
1. **`CHANGELOG.md`** (project root) — the **user-facing** changelog, the single source of truth for the notices. Keyed by `## Version X.Y.Z` headings + a `## Unreleased (next release)` section. Entries are plain "what you can see or do differently" prose — engineering/tooling changes stay out (that's what this CLAUDE.md version table is for). **This is separate from and coarser than the version table:** not every patch needs a user-facing entry, and one entry can describe a change that shipped across several patches.
2. **`scripts/apply-release-notes.mjs`** (trigger: "apply release notes") — parses `CHANGELOG.md` → injects a `const RELEASE_NOTES = {…}` object (keyed by version string) into `app/js/whats-new.js` between `@@RELEASE_NOTES_START/END@@` markers. Reads `APP_VERSION` from `app/js/app.js`; maps `## Unreleased` → `APP_VERSION` (so a dev build can preview pending notes). **The notes are BUNDLED, not fetched** — the notice must work offline / on locked-down networks, and the freshly-loaded app already *is* the new version, so it carries its own notes. Run it after every CHANGELOG.md edit; do not hand-edit the marker block. **Node.js LTS is installed on this dev box (July 9 2026, `C:\Program Files\nodejs\`), so run it directly: `node scripts/apply-release-notes.mjs` — no more hand-porting to Python.** *(Caveat: a shell/terminal launched **before** the install won't have `node` on PATH — a freshly-launched terminal, or the full path `"C:\Program Files\nodejs\node.exe"`, works. This also un-blocks the `package.json` Node tooling — `docx-js`, `puppeteer` — via `npm install`, though docx/figures currently stay on the Python toolchain by choice.)*
3. **`app/js/whats-new.js`** — `compareVersions` (semver), `collectWhatsNew(since, current)` (flat combined list, newest version first — Ken's choice), `renderWhatsNewPanel`, and `maybeShowWhatsNew(current)`. **Placement (Ken, v0.5.63 — moved out of a modal):** the announcement renders **inline into the pre-start block that overlays the transcript region** (`#whatsNewPanel`), not as a top-layer dialog — it sits in the transcript space above the centered Start button. A "Got it" button collapses the panel, leaving the Start button centered. Blue `--slot-initiative` accent card (a friendly sibling of `confirmDanger`).
4. **Storage** — `storage.loadLastSeenVersion`/`saveLastSeenVersion` in `aac_settings.lastSeenVersion`.

**Placement — Start + What's-new in the transcript region (Ken, v0.5.63).** The old full-screen `#startOverlay` (dark scrim + big centered Start) is gone. The Start button now lives in **`#startBlock`**, absolutely positioned to fill the (empty, pre-conversation) **transcript region** and flex-centered, so Start is centered in that space; the What's-new panel renders above it when an update just landed. `main.disabled` no longer dims via opacity on `main` (that would dim the Start block too, since opacity can't be un-inherited) — it blocks pointer-events and dims only the *sibling* regions (Command Bar, responses, status, dock), leaving the transcript crisp and the Start block tappable (`pointer-events: auto`). `handleStart` hides `#startBlock` (was `#startOverlay`) and the transcript becomes the live log. The Settings → About "See what's new" button was **removed** (Ken) — the notice is now purely the post-update, pre-start announcement.

**Gating (the "can't announce itself" rule):** `maybeShowWhatsNew` runs once at app load (after `initApp()`, independent of STT). If `lastSeenVersion` is **null** (brand-new install, or an app old enough to predate this feature) it just records the current version silently — the version that *introduces* the notice cannot show itself. Otherwise, if the running version is newer than `lastSeenVersion`, it collects every note in between, records the current version *before* rendering (so a reload can't re-trigger), and shows the panel.

APP_VERSION / CACHE_VERSION → **`0.5.62`** (feature) → **`0.5.63`** (inline-in-transcript placement); `whats-new.js` added to the sw precache. Also switched `.claude/launch.json` to `autoPort` (dropped the hardcoded `-l 3000`) so parallel sessions don't collide on the port. **Verified in preview (v0.5.63):** boots clean, no console errors; the Start button fills+centers in the transcript region (both axes when no panel); seeding `lastSeenVersion` older + reload renders the What's-new card inline above Start with the correct flat/newest-first 5-note list and "updated itself to version 0.5.63" intro, Start stays visible/centered-X, "Got it" collapses the card and Start recenters; the no-update path shows just the centered Start; pressing Start hides the block and enables main; the About "See what's new" button is gone. *Caveat: verifying via a real auto-update on the tablet double-loads (the SW `controllerchange` reload lands after the first render), which is correct in practice — the panel shows once the SW settles; only rapid manual `lastSeenVersion` tests need the SW already current to avoid the double-load consuming the one-shot.*

## App virtual keyboard — refinements + open design questions (June 14 2026), v0.2.11

Built (Ken's list) on top of the shared keyboard component (`app/js/keyboard.js`, `styles.css`):

1. **Keys 25% taller** — `.kbd-key min-height` 3.2rem → **4rem** (64px verified). `body.kbd-open` field clearance bumped 19rem → 23rem.
2. **Numbers + special characters on a separate page.** Two layouts: `LETTER_ROWS` (QWERTY letters + ⇧ + ⌫ + a bottom row of `123` toggle, comma, space, period, ↵) and `SYMBOL_ROWS` (digits row, two rows of specials `@#$%&*()-+` / `!?'":;/=_~`, bottom row `ABC` toggle + space + ⌫ + ↵). A `page` state + a `'page'` action key flip between them; `renderRows()` rebuilds (pointerdown handler is delegated on the root so swapping inner rows is safe). **Comma, period, space, backspace stay on the letters page per Ken;** space/backspace/enter are *also* on the symbols page because editing is impossible without them (flag if Ken wants them stripped to a strict letters-only home).
3. **Shift = one-shot, double-tap = lock.** New state machine: `'off' | 'shift' | 'lock'`. Single tap toggles off↔shift; a **double tap within 300 ms** (`SHIFT_DOUBLE_TAP_MS`) engages caps **lock**. One-shot `'shift'` auto-reverts to `'off'` after one character (`consumeShift()`); `'lock'` persists until shift is tapped again. Visuals: `.kbd-shift-on` (lighter blue, one-shot) vs `.kbd-caps` (solid dark, locked).

**NOTE — Enter key (Ken, to discuss).** `enter()` currently does newline-in-textarea / save-in-single-line. Ken flagged that **Enter may need to behave differently per context** (newline vs. save vs. speak). TODO comment left in `keyboard.js enter()`. Not yet designed. *(The "formal close/done key" half of this note is DONE — see the Hide button, v0.2.18.)*

**Hide (close) button — DONE (v0.2.18).** The toolbar above the keys now has a **Hide ⌄** button (right-aligned, dark) that dismisses the keyboard while leaving any open panel — notably **Settings** — in place (Ken: "have the keyboard close and just have the settings panel open"). `keyboard.js dismiss()`: ends preview mode, `hide()`s, and **blurs the field** so a later tap re-opens it. Works from both the typing context (API field) and the Speech-tab layout preview. Verified: Hide closes the keyboard with Settings still open and the field blurred; re-focusing the field reopens it; no console errors.

**Hide must not close About Me — DONE (v0.2.24).** Tapping the keyboard's Hide button closed the About Me screen. Cause (confirmed by hit-test): Hide runs on `pointerdown` and hides the keyboard, which frees its reserved space; the About Me header reflows to full width and the **Done** button slides into the exact spot the Hide button occupied — so the tap's trailing **click** lands on Done → `close()`. Fix: `keyboard.js dismiss()` calls `suppressNextClick()` — a one-shot capturing `click` listener that swallows the ghost click (auto-removed on first click or after 400 ms). Verified: the ghost click targeted `worldviewCloseBtn` and was `defaultPrevented`; About Me stayed open, keyboard hid; a later legitimate Done click still closes it. (General gotcha: hiding the keyboard on pointerdown reflows the page, so anything that slides under the pointer can catch the trailing click.)

**Reshow on tapping a layout setting — DONE (v0.2.19).** Tapping any keyboard-layout control (side/bottom layout selects, side-position slider) reshows the keyboard if it was hidden via Hide. The trigger is **`onpointerdown`** (not just `onfocus`), because the common case is re-tapping a select that *still has focus* after Hide — where no `focus` event fires. pointerdown fires on every tap regardless of focus state; the bottom select previews the bottom dock, the side controls the side dock.

**RESOLVED — keyboard layout (Ken, June 14 2026): Alphabetical only, QWERTY dropped.** The target users aren't touch-typists, so QWERTY had no value; **A–Z order is easiest to scan.** Shipped as the single layout (`LETTER_ROWS` = a–i / j–r / s–z+⌫ / ⇧·123·,·space·.·↵). **No layout Settings option yet** — other layouts (e.g. frequency-based) are a **later phase**, at which point layout becomes a user setting; the rows stay pure data so adding one is data, not code. (Matrix/grid layouts remain the natural future fit for scanning/eye-gaze renderers, but that's later.)

**RESOLVED — keyboard docking is CONTEXT-based, not layout- or (yet) orientation-based (Ken, June 14 2026).** Correction to the earlier note: **don't tie docking to layout.** The dock follows the *screen*, not the device orientation (for now):
- **Side dock** — **Settings & About Me** (those screens compete for *horizontal* space and want full height). Implemented: full-height right-side column, `width: min(26rem, 50vw)`; the About Me content reserves `padding-right` to clear it. (Currently the app keyboard is in scope only for About Me's `.wv-text`; Settings still uses the OS keyboard pending the open question below, but is grouped here for when/if it switches.)
- **Bottom dock** — **during conversations** (the composer; competes for *vertical* space). Implemented: full-width bottom strip, `main`/composer reserves `padding-bottom`.
- Mechanism: `dockFor(field)` in `keyboard.js` (`.wv-text` → side, else bottom) sets `kbd-dock-side`/`kbd-dock-bottom` on the root + `body`; CSS positions the panel and reserves the right axis of clearance per dock. Left-vs-right side is a future config knob.
- **Dynamic / orientation-aware docking (switch by landscape vs. portrait) is a LATER refinement**, not built. (The original "scarce axis rotates with the device" reasoning still holds for that future step.)
- **Access-method framing:** dock placement is just a **renderer** choice over the same key set — consistent with the engine-emits-descriptor / renderer-presents invariant.

**RESOLVED — Settings uses the app keyboard, side-docked (Ken, June 14 2026), v0.2.14.** The Settings API-key field is now in the keyboard's `IN_SCOPE` (`#apiKeyInput`), so onscreen mode sets `inputmode="none"` (Windows keyboard suppressed) and the app's own keyboard appears, **side-docked** (`dockFor` returns side for `#settingsDialog` fields). **Top-layer fix:** the Settings dialog is a `showModal()` dialog (top layer, makes normal-flow content inert), so a body-appended keyboard would render behind it and be dead. `show()` therefore **reparents the keyboard into the open dialog** (`field.closest('dialog[open]')`) so it shares the dialog's top layer and stays interactive; it reparents back to `<body>` for the composer/worldview. The keyboard is `position:fixed` and the dialog sets no containing block (drag uses `left/top`, not `transform`) and `overflow:hidden` doesn't clip fixed descendants, so it still docks to the screen edge. *Trade-off accepted:* the app keyboard loses OS copy/paste/autofill for the API key — Ken chose the app keyboard anyway (a paste affordance could be added later). *Overlap:* a centered dialog and the side keyboard overlap (the field's far side sits under the keys); the **draggable panel** clears it (the reason it was made draggable). Auto-repositioning the dialog clear of the keyboard is a possible later polish; not done (a `transform` shift would break the fixed keyboard's edge docking, so any auto-shift must use `left`/`margin`).

This also **moots the old "panel + Windows keyboard can't both fit vertically" issue** — there's no Windows keyboard in Settings anymore, and the side dock leaves the panel its full height. (The constant panel size from v0.2.11, `#settingsDialog { height: min(30rem, calc(100vh - 3rem)) }`, stays.)

**Auto-nudge the panel clear of the keyboard — now CSS-driven (v0.2.15, reworked v0.2.20).** When a **side** keyboard is showing, the centred Settings panel re-centres into the free region beside it. **v0.2.20** moved this from JS (`updateSettingsNudge`, removed) to **pure CSS keyed off the keyboard's `body` classes** — `body.kbd-open.kbd-dock-side.kbd-side-right #settingsDialog { right: min(26rem,46vw); max-width: … }` and the `.kbd-side-left` mirror. Why: the JS version keyed off the *slider position* and only ran on open/mode/slider change, so after **previewing a bottom keyboard then switching to side** the nudge went stale (Ken's repro). CSS keyed off the actual keyboard state is automatically correct — nudges only when a side keyboard is really shown, on the right side, and **not** for a bottom keyboard or a hidden one. The **`max-width: min(560px, calc(100vw - min(26rem,46vw) - 2rem))`** cap fixes the keyboard-on-left case that pushed the panel's right edge **off-screen** (it only bit when `viewport − keyboard < panel width`, i.e. windowed/narrow — hence intermittent). A **dragged** panel keeps its inline `left/top` (drag wins). Verified on the narrow (812px) preview: bottom-preview → panel centred (no nudge); side-left → panel right & on-screen; side-right → panel left & on-screen.

**Settings panel + keyboard — three fixes (v0.2.21, from Ken's 7-step repro).**
1. **No keyboard race on tab switch.** A modal `<dialog>` autofocuses its first focusable control — the API-key field — which popped the keyboard in *real-typing* mode on open; tapping the Speech & Input tab then raced a blur/hide against the preview-show, so the keyboard could vanish. Fix: `#settingsHeader` is `tabindex="-1"` and `openSettings()` focuses it after `showModal()`, so the dialog opens with **no keyboard**; it appears only when a field or a layout setting is tapped. (The harness couldn't reproduce the race — its window isn't OS-focused so the autofocus never popped the keyboard there — but removing the autofocus removes the root cause.)
2. **Bottom keyboard pushes the panel up.** A bottom keyboard covers the lower screen, so `body.kbd-open.kbd-dock-bottom #settingsDialog { top: 1rem; bottom: auto; max-height: calc(52vh - 1.5rem) }` pins the panel to the top region (was left centred, overlapping the keys).
3. **Dragged panel re-snaps on keyboard change.** Dragging pins the panel with inline `left/top` (overriding the CSS nudge), so a later side switch overlapped. `keyboard.js setDock()` now dispatches a `kbd-dock-change` event when the effective dock/side actually changes; `app.js` `resetSettingsPosition()` clears the inline drag (`settingsDragged` flag) on that event, on Close, and on cancel — so the panel re-snaps to the auto-position that clears the keyboard. Drag still works *within* a stable keyboard state.

Verified in preview: open → header focused, no keyboard; tap API field → keyboard shows + typing works; bottom layout → panel pinned top, clears the keys; drag then switch to a side layout → inline position cleared, panel re-snaps clear of the side keyboard; no console errors.

**About Me content width (v0.2.20 → reverted v0.2.22).** History: cards were full-viewport-width; v0.2.20 capped to a 40rem centred column to make them "thinner." **v0.2.22:** Ken wanted the questions to **fill the available width** (a 40rem column left big empty margins on a wide window, and didn't fill the space beside the keyboard). Removed the `max-width`/`margin:auto` cap — `#worldviewContent` now fills its container minus a reasonable side margin (`padding: 1.25rem 2rem 3rem`). Because the side-keyboard reservation lives on **`#worldviewScreen` padding** (= `min(26rem,46vw)`, the keyboard width), the content box is already "window − keyboard", so the questions fill **all** the non-keyboard space automatically (and the full window when no keyboard is shown) — no JS measurement needed. Verified at 1600px: no keyboard → content 0–1600 (cards ~1521); keyboard-left → content 416–1600; keyboard-right → content 0–1184.

**Scrollbar — gap + width (v0.2.23, Ken).** The scroll container's right-edge scrollbar hugged a **right-side** keyboard (its right margin is *padding*, inside the scrollbar), so reaching for it risked hitting a key. Fix: the keyboard-on-right `#worldviewScreen` reservation gets an extra **`+ 1.5rem`** so the scroll container (and its scrollbar) sits a gap away from the keys (keyboard-on-left is unaffected — scrollbar stays far right). Verified gap = 24px between scrollbar and keyboard. **Wide scrollbars:** `#worldviewContent`/`#settingsContent` `::-webkit-scrollbar { width: 22px }` with a high-contrast thumb — Chromium-only (the Surface browsers), far easier to grab for limited-motor-control users. Tunable.

**Layout live-preview on the Speech & Input tab (v0.2.17).** Problem (Ken): the side keyboard vanished when switching to the Speech & Input tab — exactly when you want to watch it as you change layouts — because the keyboard is tied to a focused text field and that tab has none (only selects + the slider). Fix: a **preview mode** in `keyboard.js` (`previewing` flag + `previewShow(dock)`/`previewHide()`): shows the keyboard with no `activeField` (keys are inert), hosted in the open Settings dialog (top layer). The `focusout` handler returns early while `previewing` so blurring the API field to click a tab doesn't hide it. `app.js`: activating the Speech & Input tab (in onscreen mode) calls `previewShow('side')`; focusing/changing a layout select previews that dock (`previewShow('bottom'|'side')`) and re-renders; leaving the tab or `setMode('physical')` calls `previewHide()`. **Cleanup gotcha:** the dialog `close` event proved unreliable here (fired by neither programmatic `close()` nor observed), so dismissal cleanup (clear nudge + `previewHide`) is done **explicitly in the Close handler** and via a **`cancel`** listener for Escape — not the `close` event. Verified in preview: keyboard stays on the Speech tab and updates on layout/side change; hides on tab-away, Close, and cancel/Escape; real API-field typing still works.

**Cut / Copy / Paste toolbar (v0.2.15).** A persistent `.kbd-toolbar` (Cut, Copy, Paste) sits above the keys, **layout-independent** (built once in `keyboard.js` `build()`; `renderRows()` clears only `.kbd-row` so the toolbar survives layout/page switches). Buttons act on `pointerdown`+`preventDefault` (field keeps focus/selection) → `handleTool()` uses the async Clipboard API: copy/cut `writeText(selection)` (cut also deletes the selection); paste `readText()` → `insert()` at the caret. This restores the clipboard affordances the OS keyboard would have given — **paste in particular for the long `sk-ant-…` API key**. *Clipboard needs a focused document + user gesture (both true in real use); it's blocked in the unfocused preview harness, so cut/copy/paste are verified for wiring (Cut removes the selection) but the actual clipboard round-trip must be confirmed on the tablet.*

APP_VERSION / CACHE_VERSION → `0.2.11`. **Verified in preview** (on-screen mode, composer focused): keys 64px tall; letters page has no digit row + a `123` toggle; symbols page shows digits + specials + `ABC`/space/⌫/↵; one-shot shift capitalizes one letter then reverts (`Ab`); double-tap engages caps lock (`CD` stays upper); single tap (after >300 ms) unlocks; page toggles both ways; no console errors.

### Selectable layouts + window-aware docking (v0.2.13)

The twenty layouts from `Keyboard-Layout-Options.docx` are now **implemented as data and user-selectable**.
- **`app/js/keyboard-layouts.js`** — all 20 layouts (S1–S10 side, B1–B10 bottom) encoded as rows of typed cells (`char`/`space`/`action`/`blank`/`pred`), each cell carrying a `span` (= flex weight, so any row width fills the keyboard and wide keys keep their proportions). Plus a per-dock `SYMBOLS` page (side 5-wide / bottom 10-wide) for the 123 key, and ordered `SIDE_LAYOUTS`/`BOTTOM_LAYOUTS` lists for the menus. **Layout is data, not code** — adding one (e.g. a future frequency layout) is a new entry here.
- **`keyboard.js`** — renders the selected layout for the current dock; `renderRows()` is span-aware and renders `blank`/`pred` cells as inert fillers. New API: `setSideLayout`/`setBottomLayout`/`setSideDockPosition`. The side dock now positions left **or** right (`kbd-side-left`/`kbd-side-right`).
- **Three Settings (Speech & Input tab):** "Bottom-docked keyboard layout" (select, default B1), "Side-docked keyboard layout" (select, default S1), and a binary **Left/Right slider** for the side dock (default Right). Stored in `aac_settings` (`bottomLayout`/`sideLayout`/`sideDockPosition`); `storage.js` accessors; live-applied on change and persisted on Save; set on init.
- **Docking is still context-fixed for now:** conversation → bottom, About Me → side (Ken: revisit conditions after more conversation-UI design).

**Layout-independent fixes (the v0.2.12 side-dock problems Ken flagged):**
- **Side dock fills its column** — rows `flex:1` (removed the `justify-content:center` that left empty bands above/below). Keys size from row height. Fixes wasted vertical space + tiny keys.
- **About Me uses all remaining horizontal space** — removed `#worldviewContent`'s `max-width:800px`/centering; it now fills the width, and only the keyboard's docked side is reserved (`padding-left`/`-right`).
- **Conversation field stays visible** — bottom dock caps at `48vh` and the content reserves `padding-bottom:50vh`, so the focused composer scrolls clear above the keys (verified: composer bottom above keyboard top).
- **Both docks are window-size aware** (full-screen or windowed): key heights `clamp(2.2rem,7.5vh,4rem)`, side width `min(26rem,46vw)`, bottom cap `48vh`, reservations in `vh`/`vw`.

APP_VERSION / CACHE_VERSION → `0.2.13`. **Verified in preview:** Settings shows the 3 controls (10+10 options, slider); selecting B3 renders the 3-row 13-wide bottom keyboard; About Me side dock fills the column height and renders S1, content reserves the keyboard side and otherwise fills width; switching to S6 + Left re-renders the 11-row layout docked left with the reservation flipped; composer stays above the bottom keyboard; no console errors. *Touch + real-window behavior to confirm on the tablet.*

## Settings Panel — usability pass (June 14 2026), v0.2.8

Six Settings-panel improvements shipped (Ken's list), modeled on the Keyguard Designer web app's settings panel (`keyguard-designer-web/app.html`, the second working directory). *(Superseded in v0.2.16: the **Save button was removed** — every control now applies AND persists immediately; the "Save persists / Close keeps for the session" wording in items 1 and 3 below is historical.)*

1. **"Cancel" → "Close".** Footer button relabeled (`index.html`). Behavior unchanged — it dismisses without persisting; **Save** persists.
2. **Draggable modal.** Added a dark title-bar `#settingsHeader` ("Settings" + faint "drag to move" hint) as the drag handle. `initSettingsDrag()` in `app.js` (adapted from Keyguard's `mousedown` drag, but using **pointer events** + `setPointerCapture`): on first drag it converts the native `<dialog>`'s UA centering to pixel `left/top` with `margin:0`, then tracks the pointer clamped to the viewport. Stays modal — the backdrop still blocks the app behind it — but can be moved aside. Position persists for the session.
3. **Live-apply of UI/behavior settings while the modal is open.** Settings that change app behavior now take effect on `change`, not only on Save, so (with the panel dragged aside) the effect is visible/audible immediately — the pattern Keyguard uses. Wired in `openSettings()` via `.onchange`: voice (`tts.setVoice`), silence threshold (`stt.setSilenceThreshold`), keyboard mode (`keyboard.setMode`). Save persists to storage; Close keeps the live changes for the session. This is the **foundation for future UI-customization settings** Ken flagged as coming.
4. **Point-release version in About.** `APP_VERSION` constant in `app.js` (currently `0.2.8`) injected into `#aboutVersion`; About now reads "Version 0.2.8 — Phase 1 Proof of Concept". **Bump `APP_VERSION` alongside `sw.js` CACHE_VERSION every release** — crucial for beta-tester bug reports.
5. **Scrolling panel.** Dialog is a flex **column** (header + footer pinned via `flex-shrink:0`, `#settingsContent` scrolling via `flex:1; min-height:0` on `#settingsLayout`, `max-height:calc(100vh - 3rem)`). **Two gotchas (the v0.2.8→0.2.9 fix below):** (a) `display:flex` on a `<dialog>` **drops the UA's auto-centering** (computed margin → 0, pins top-left) — restored with `inset:0; margin:auto` on the base `dialog`. (b) Putting `display:flex` on the *base* `dialog` selector also **overrode the UA's `dialog:not([open]) { display:none }`** (author origin beats UA regardless of specificity), so the panel was CSS-visible at all times — it showed at startup *and* "Close" appeared to do nothing (the button handlers are wired in `openSettings()`, which never ran, and `.close()` is a no-op on a not-truly-open dialog). **Fix:** scope the flex layout to **`dialog[open]`** so a closed dialog stays `display:none`.
6. **General tab shortened (Ken's call: "add Speech + Conversation tabs").** Tabs are now **General** (API Key, About You pointer, Data Folder) / **Speech & Input** (Voice, Keyboard for typing) / **Conversation** (silence period, auto-resume, + the former Placeholders delays folded in) / **About**. The standalone Placeholders tab is gone. Tab-switching JS is generic (`data-tab`), so no handler changes were needed.

**Touch drag:** `#settingsHeader` has `touch-action: none` so the browser doesn't claim the touch gesture for scroll/pan (without it a touch drag moved the panel only a few mm).

CACHE_VERSION → `aac-v0.2.9`; APP_VERSION → `0.2.9`. **Verified in preview:** panel hidden at startup (`display:none`, not in DOM box); opens to `display:flex` via the button; **Close actually closes** (`open:false`, `display:none`); tabs distribute correctly; version 0.2.9 in About; drag moves precisely and clamps to viewport edges; centered on default open; long (Conversation) tab scrolls on a short viewport with footer pinned; no console errors. *Note:* drag + `touch-action` tested via synthetic PointerEvents (harness window isn't OS-focused and synthetic events bypass `touch-action`) — **confirm real touch drag on the test tablet.**

### Conversation-pane layout fixes: cross-region scrollbar, wide scrollbar, bubble width (Ken, July 17 2026)

Three conversation-screen fixes (dev 0.5.95, CSS-only, `styles.css`):
1. **Momentary cross-region scrollbar + width jump on card select (root cause).** `#mainArea` — the flex column holding the transcript **and** the Command Bar **and** the response palette — had `overflow: auto`. Appending a turn on a card tap momentarily overflowed it during reflow, so a transient vertical scrollbar spanned all three regions, shrinking their width and jerking the command buttons + cards left. Changed to **`overflow: clip`** so `#mainArea` never paints a scrollbar; the transcript (`.transcript-log`, its own `overflow-y: auto`) is the only intended scroll container. Verified: with 40 turns overflowing, `#mainArea` scrollbar = 0px and the Command Bar / response area stay full width (1280px), while the transcript scrolls internally.
2. **Transcript scrollbar too narrow for motor control.** Added the v0.2.23 wide-scrollbar treatment (`::-webkit-scrollbar { width: 22px }` + high-contrast thumb) to `.transcript-log` — it's the only scroll surface during a conversation. Verified 22px when overflowing.
3. **User-statement background stretched ~80% across.** `.turn` was a block div with `max-width: 88%`, so a short user turn showed a wide blue box with the text hugging the right and empty colour to its left. Added **`width: fit-content`** so each turn's coloured bubble shrinks to its text — proper chat bubbles (partner hugs left, user hugs right), `max-width: 88%` still wrapping long turns. Verified: short user turn 60px on the right, partner 210px on the left.

Verified in preview (both fixes are layout-mode-agnostic; tested bottom dock); no console errors. Not version-bumped (0.5.95 dev cycle).

### API-key feedback: missing-key prompt, format warning, live Test button (Ken, July 17 2026)

**Origin:** Ken (testing the 0.5.93 deploy) asked why there's no visible message when no API key is set, and why pasting an incomplete key (missing 3 chars) showed no error. Checking the **User Manual §3.2** settled it: the manual *promises* "a red warning about an invalid key" at the bottom of the General tab — **which was never built** (no key validation existed at all), and the code's "No API key set…" message was routed through the **visually-hidden status bar** (v0.5.2), so it never showed. Ken: build all three surfaces.

**Built (dev 0.5.94; not a new subsystem — patch-level):**
1. **Format warning under the API Key field** (`#apiKeyStatus`, Settings → General) — `llm.validateKeyFormat(key)` (no network) flags the gross paste mistakes: missing `sk-ant-` prefix, embedded whitespace, or too short (<40). Red note as you type; clears when empty (empty = *missing*, not *invalid*) or well-formed. Realizes the manual's promised red warning.
2. **"Test" button** beside Paste — `llm.testApiKey(key)` does a live **`GET /v1/models?limit=1`** (authenticates the key but **bills no tokens**) with the `anthropic-dangerous-direct-browser-access` header: 200 → "✓ Your key is working"; 401/403 → "✗ The key was rejected — check you copied all of it"; else → "couldn't reach the service." **This is the only surface that catches a subtly-wrong/truncated key** (Anthropic keys have no fixed public length, so the format check can't). *Verified in preview that `/v1/models` works with browser CORS from localhost — a bogus well-formed key returns 401 → "rejected."*
3. **Pre-start "no API key" prompt** (`#apiKeyPrompt` in `#startBlock`, over the transcript region like the What's-new panel) — amber "action needed" card shown when no key is saved, with an "Open Settings to add your key" button (→ `openSettings`). Informational, not blocking (the user can still Start and use manual AAC — the AI-optional property). `refreshApiKeyPrompt()` shows/hides it on load and whenever the key field changes.

**Files:** `llm.js` (`validateKeyFormat` + `testApiKey`); `app.js` (`showApiKeyStatus`/`reflectApiKeyFormat`/`refreshApiKeyPrompt`, Test-button + prompt-button wiring, oninput now reflects format + prompt, `initApp` calls `refreshApiKeyPrompt`); `index.html` (Test button, `#apiKeyStatus`, `#apiKeyPrompt`); `styles.css` (status line + amber prompt card + Test button). No version bump yet (0.5.94 dev cycle). **Verified in preview:** prompt visible with no key / hidden once a key is present / re-shown when cleared; all three format warnings fire and clear on a well-formed key; live Test returns "rejected" for a bogus key; 119/119 tests; no console errors. **Docs follow-up (deferred to a "sync docs"):** the User Manual §3.2 wording ("bottom of the General tab") is slightly off (the warning is directly under the field) and doesn't yet mention the **Test button** or the **pre-start prompt** — update on the next manual sync. *(The manual's promised warning now exists, so §3.2 is no longer describing vaporware — but it undersells the new Test button, the real fix for a truncated key.)*

### Wind-down and closing are two distinct steps; static palettes paginate (Ken, July 17 2026)

**Decision (Ken):** "Wind down" and "closing" are separate CA closing-sequence steps and must be separate card categories. **Wind-down statements** signal an intent to end *without* saying goodbye ("I should get going.", "Great catching up with you."); **closing statements** are the actual goodbyes ("Bye!", "Take care!"). This splits the old single mixed `closers` list into two.

**Flow built:**
- **Wind down** button → always shows the **wind-down** statements (was: the mixed closers).
- Selecting a wind-down statement → **speaks it, then automatically offers the closings (goodbyes).** So the user winds down first, then signs off.
- Selecting a closing → re-offers the closings (a partner may say bye back). Partner-initiated closing (AI `CLOSING` classification, or the `looksLikeClosing` fast-path) also shows the goodbyes so the user reciprocates.
- **If the partner doesn't reciprocate**, pressing **Wind down** again re-offers the wind-downs — Ken: no *escalation*, just "a different 4" (page to the next set of the defined wind-downs).

**Pagination of static palettes (Ken's "does the New-4 button work for wind-downs?" → yes, and it extends to all three static sets).** Openers / wind-downs / closings are predefined lists that can define MORE cards than the footprint shows (4, or 8 in 2-per-category mode). The user pages through the extras two ways, both a "dip into the pre-defined set" (no LLM):
- the **"New N"** regenerate button, when a static palette is showing, dips to the next page (wrapping) instead of calling the AI — for openers, wind-downs, AND closings;
- **re-pressing Wind down** advances the wind-down page (first press of a conversation shows page 0; each re-press dips further, wrapping).
Per-kind offsets persist for the conversation and reset at Start/End. When normal AI response cards show, the static cursor is cleared so "New N" regenerates (doesn't page).

**Code:** `SLOT.WIND_DOWN` added (`engine.js`/`ui.js` — slate "wrapping-up" hue, shares CLOSING's color since they never co-display). `engine.js`: `DEFAULT_CLOSERS` → `DEFAULT_WIND_DOWNS` + `DEFAULT_CLOSINGS`; `windDown()` now returns the wind-down palette; new `showClosings()` returns the goodbyes; `setConversationPhrases({openers, windDowns, closings})` (still accepts a legacy `closers` as the wind-downs). `control-phrases.js`: `closers` → `windDowns` + `closings` (defaults, `normalize`, `mergeNewDefaults`, `seeded` watermark, reset). **Legacy migration:** a file with the old single `closers` list and no new keys simply reseeds both new lists from defaults (the two senses can't be auto-classified) and the old list is ignored — single-user pre-beta precedent; re-edit via Settings → Controls if the old closers were customized. `control-phrases-editor.js`: the one "Closings" section becomes two — **Wind-down statements** + **Closings (goodbyes)**. `app.js`: `renderStaticPalette`/`pageWindow`/`staticOffsets`/`resetStaticPaging`, `offerClosings` (replaces `reofferClosings`), `handleWindDown`/`handleInitiate`/`handleRegenerate` + the CLOSING paths route through the pager. **Verified in preview** (static flows need no API key): Wind down → wind-downs p0; New-4 → wind-downs p1; select one → goodbyes auto-appear ("Say goodbye, or wait for their reply"); New-4 → goodbyes p1; re-press Wind down cycles wind-down pages; openers page via New-4; End→Start resets paging to p0; 119/119 unit tests, no console errors. Not version-bumped (patch-level work on the current 0.5.93 dev cycle; committed locally per the release rule when Ken directs).

## Versioning

Format: **major.minor.patch** (e.g., `0.3.8`). All pre-release versions use major `0`.

**When to bump which digit (agreed Ken, June 18 2026):**
- **Patch (third digit)** — the default for almost everything: bug fixes, refinements to existing behavior, and small self-contained features that *extend* the existing feature set. Mental test: "Is this changing or fixing how an existing capability works?" → patch.
- **Minor (second digit)** — reserved for a substantial **new architectural layer or subsystem** that later work builds on (e.g. `0.2` continuous partner capture; `0.3.0` the Conversation Engine). The bar is deliberately high: "new layer," not just "new feature" — the worldview questionnaire and relationship graph shipped as `0.2.x` patches because they slotted into the existing app rather than restructuring it. Roughly aligns with crossing a Phase boundary or adding a Phase's defining capability. Mental test: "Did this add a genuinely new subsystem the app didn't have before?" → minor.
- **Major (`1.0` and beyond)** — **Ken's call alone** (Ken, June 18 2026), based on maturity and readiness. Claude Code does not make the 0.x→1.0 transition without Ken explicitly directing it.

**Release process — the PC is dev, GitHub is release (Ken, July 4 2026; formalized in [RELEASING.md](RELEASING.md)).** This **supersedes** the earlier "commit and push every completed step" rule — testers are coming online, so we are now conservative about what reaches GitHub. The full ritual lives in `RELEASING.md`; the essentials:
- **Commit to local `main` = save work; push `main` = release.** A GitHub Actions workflow deploys `app/` to Pages on **every** push to `main`, so **any** push (even docs-only) reaches users. Between releases Claude **commits each change locally but does NOT push** — local commits sync/back up via OneDrive. There is one branch (`main`); no `dev` branch.
- **Changelog-as-you-go.** Every likely-**user-visible** change gets a plain-English bullet under `## Unreleased (next release)` in `CHANGELOG.md`, **in the same commit as the code**, written the way a *target user* reads it (not engineering language). `CHANGELOG.md` is the single source of truth for the in-app "What's new" notes (via `scripts/apply-release-notes.mjs`).
- **Pre-bump.** At the end of each release the dev copy is pre-bumped to the **next patch** (`APP_VERSION` + `CACHE_VERSION`), so the dev About screen always reads higher than the last public release and you know you're on a dev build. It's provisional (local, unpushed); the **final digit is decided at release**.
- **~~iPad work is pushed live but NOT announced~~ → RETIRED at 0.6.0; there is no longer a `## Held back` section, and there should not be one again (Ken, August 3 2026).** *"I assume that since iPad support is now part of the official release there will no longer be a ## Held back section."* Correct. The arrangement existed only because iPad work had to reach the device by a push that also deployed to Windows users, while the iPad was still an experiment nobody had been told about; **0.6.0 made iPad a supported platform**, so there is nothing left to withhold. It wound down cleanly — both flagged entries, **Export/Import** and the **API-key Paste button**, were announced in the 0.6.0 notes (verified in `CHANGELOG.md`), so nothing was parked and forgotten. **Platform-specific notes are now handled the right way instead**, by the `### On an iPad` / `### On a computer` scoping the generator understands: each user sees what applies to them plus one line saying the other device had improvements too — announced but not confusing, rather than silent. *The `## Held back` stop-condition stays in `RELEASING.md` as a general guard (it costs nothing and would catch a future deliberate withholding), but it is not in use and a release that finds one should treat it as a question, not a routine.* **Historical mechanism, if it is ever needed again:** `apply-release-notes.mjs` ignores every H2 except `## Version X.Y.Z` and `## Unreleased`, so notes parked under any other heading are kept as a record and never bundled.
- **Trigger phrase "bump Conversant AAC"** — or **"bump Conversant"**, the original short form; both mean the same thing (Ken only, after he's verified `CHANGELOG.md`). Runs the whole ritual **through the push**: finalize the digit → rename `## Unreleased` to `## Version <final>` → regenerate the bundled notes → add the version-table row below → commit → push (deploys) → pre-bump the next cycle.
  - **Prefer the trigger over ad-hoc wording** ("let's push the latest code" — Ken, Aug 1 2026). The value is sequencing: an ad-hoc instruction leaves it ambiguous whether the full ritual is wanted, and the ritual has an order that is easy to get wrong (the iPad-publish-before-pre-bump trap below was hit exactly that way).
  - **What it decides on its own, so "no second confirmation" is honest:** the version is the **pre-bumped patch digit** already in `APP_VERSION`, and the release announces **everything under `## Unreleased`**, unchanged.
  - **The only two things that stop and ask**, because they are Ken's calls and were both live on Aug 1 2026: (a) the cycle added a genuinely new **subsystem**, so the minor digit is arguably due rather than the patch — propose it with the reasoning; (b) a **`## Held back`** section exists in `CHANGELOG.md`, so something is deliberately unannounced and its disposition has to be settled. Neither is normally present.
- **Docs get restamped after the push, not before.** `DOC-SYNC.md`'s `At commit` must name a commit that exists on `origin/main` (see the ordering rule at the top of that file), so any documentation synced during the dev cycle is restamped at the release commit once the push lands. The CLAUDE.md version table is **released-versions-only** (rows added at bump, not during dev).

(2) **Closed-set (alternative-question) turns** — classification gains `offered_options`; when the partner offers a menu the four structural slots are abandoned and the palette becomes one `CHOICE` card per alternative, with every spare cell filled from a ranked filler set (`CHOICE_OTHER` ×2 / `CHOICE_ASK` / `CHOICE_REPAIR`) so a two-way question doesn't leave dead cells. The trigger covers both alternative questions AND the far commoner list-in-a-statement form ("we've got muffins, croissants…"), guarded against narrative lists; the partner's own alternatives always outrank fillers.
