(4) **Steering now survives "New N"** within a turn (chip focus and typed Reframe text both), fixing a silent reset. (5) **Live partner speech stays in view** while they talk — `setTranscriptState`/`setNowPlaying` changed the pane's height after the scroll. (6) **`CLOSING` defined in the prompt** (empty discourse markers, warrants, arrangements, appreciations — with a topic-vs-conversation guard) plus a pinned **"Actually, before you go —"** card and `engine.reopenFromClosing()`. (7) **The listening chime is once per conversation** when auto-resume is on. 155 unit tests; live-model verified for the offered-set classification. |
| 0.6.0   | 1     | **iPad becomes a supported platform, and speech gets paid backends (Ken, Aug 1 2026).** The minor digit, because the cycle added genuinely new subsystems rather than features: two paid speech backends reached **browser-direct with no server** (Deepgram streaming transcription, gated by `vad.js` so silence is not billed; Deepgram Aura voice, with a repeated-phrase cache), a **second storage backend** (OPFS behind the same file-handle interfaces, so `storage.js` is unchanged above the handle), and the **`platform.js` layer** that lets one codebase serve both browser engines with every iOS-only value resolving to the previous Chromium value on desktop. Settings reorganized: a new **Speech** tab holding the Deepgram key + the two independent provider choices + both voice pickers, "Speech & Input" renamed **Buttons & Keyboard**, and the footer **Close** button replaced by an **X** in the title bar (~55px back to every tab). Storage wording made platform-neutral. Announced as one iPad summary rather than fifteen itemized fixes (Ken's call). Documents restructured the same day: Product Overview unified, User Manual split into two self-contained manuals, and the iPad Architecture document merged into the Architecture Overview with its probe evidence split into Platform Measurements. |
| 0.6.2   | 1     | **Spoken help in Settings, Express-derived control sizing, and a screen edge margin for keyguards (Ken, August 2 2026).** (1) **Spoken help** — a `?` in the Settings title bar arms "tap a control to hear what it does" (`help-mode.js`; 85 phrases authored for LISTENING in a root `settings-help.json` embedded at build time, like `CHANGELOG.md`). The affordance **Rule 14** was missing: a tooltip needs hover, which direct select cannot produce, and audio costs no panel space. An armed tap is intercepted in the capture phase so it can never change the control; any tap barges out, and tapping the SAME control also passes through — except on a slider, where the tap coordinate IS the value. One-shot, so the user is never trapped in help mode. Voice = the practice partner's, on both TTS backends: intelligibility over distinctness (Ken — on an iPad "any voice that is not theirs" can be a joke voice). Coverage, orphans, drift and phrase length are all enforced by `tests/settings-help.test.mjs`. (2) **Settings controls sized off the Express Panel button** (**Rule 15**) — 70% of a cell, floored at `--btn-min-dim`: "Copy" had been 35 px wide and every `<select>` 33 px tall, matched by none of the button rules. Extended to the title-bar `?`/X, reversing their exemption, because the likely mis-hit became Help → Close, which dismisses the panel. (3) **"Screen edge margin"** (**Rule 16**) — insets the whole app INCLUDING the dock, for a keyguard inside a tight case opening; the solver budgets against the reduced viewport so regions shrink into it rather than overflowing. (4) **Two false-alarm fixes**: the "storage warm-up did not finish" error logged on EVERY start (`Promise.race` does not cancel the loser and the timer was never cleared), and log text is now scrubbed to plain ASCII at the `logError` choke point (punctuation only — an error can carry a person's name). (5) The **"Voices offered to the app"** readout was removed from About, its diagnostic question having been settled. New `tests/styles.test.mjs` tripwire after a stray `*/` silently dropped a whole CSS rule and collapsed Rule 15 to its floor with no error anywhere. |
| 0.6.7   | 1     | **Where you are is the SETTING, not the subject (Ken, August 5 2026).** Found in the demo script, which tapped the comic shop and then treated the resulting comics talk as the feature working: the place button stands in for the GPS we do not have, and it had drifted into a way to frame the topic. The prompt never said otherwise, and the fact list leaked in that direction on its own — details with no stated purpose read as material to work in, the same defect found in the privacy blocks in August. **The correction was then over-corrected and fixed again**, which is the part worth keeping: the first cut gated the facts behind "only when the partner raises it", a clause borrowed from the privacy blocks where it is right and here is wrong. Those facts are not private, they are **ambient**, and withholding them costs exactly the competence the feature exists to give. Ken’s formulation is now the prompt nearly verbatim — *the purpose of the place should inform the suggestions, but it should not become the topic of discussion* — with his own failure example quoted in it ("what did you find here last Saturday?", asked while standing in the shop), since a concrete failure instructs better than a rule. His "a recorded fact is meaningless when you are standing in it" also exposed a **second bug**: the current place was being sent **twice** under two contradicting framings, and the standing "Places I go — When: Saturdays" entry is precisely what generates the last-Saturday question, so `buildBlock` now omits the place the user is in. The partner button carries the matching line (the person being spoken TO, not a topic to raise). Framing keeps its own channel: it arrives through Reframe today, and through context-scoped Express Panel buttons later. |
| 0.6.6   | 1     | **The Listen button stops losing the partner's words (Ken, August 5 2026).** Two fixes to the same symptom, found from opposite ends. (1) **The `capturing` fix** — `handleSttStatus` treated every unrecognized status as *not listening*, silently; the paid backend emits `'capturing'` when the voice gate opens, so the indicator went dark exactly when it was most true, and the next tap took the START branch. It is now a whitelist: only an explicit `stopped`/`error` turns listening off, and an unknown status leaves the state alone and is logged. (2) **Stop/start mid-turn is a PAUSE, not a turn boundary** — `stt.js` splits the mic-opening into `openSource()`, shared by `startListening()` (which resets the transcript) and a new `resumeListening()` (which does not); `app.js` takes the resume path via `resumePartnerCapture()` when `heardPartnerText()` is non-empty. **The invariant: a partner turn ends at a FLOOR CHANGE — a response picked, a pardon, or end of conversation — never at a mic toggle.** Diagnosed from Ken's scripted `LISTEN-TEST.md` run: the saved conversation file kept the *first* checkpoint's timestamp while carrying the *second* checkpoint's text, proving the record was reused and overwritten rather than re-created, so the loss was both a cleared buffer and a shortened line. Pinned by `tests/stt.test.mjs` ("LISTEN-TEST Test 3 == Test 4"). (3) **The diagnostic trace was added AND removed inside this cycle** and never reached a release — Ken's call, to be revisited pre-beta as a Troubleshooting tab against whatever the engine has become by then; see Open Questions. |
| 0.6.5   | 1     | **Settings stays in front of fullscreen; "Use the whole screen" withdrawn on iPad; and the model is told its words are SPOKEN and must not be coarse (Ken, August 3 2026).** (1) Toggling fullscreen from inside Settings drew the whole conversation screen OVER the panel — not z-index but the **top layer**, which paints in the order things were added to it: `showModal()` promoted Settings, the toggle then promoted `document.documentElement` above it. Ken's own "closing and reopening resets it" was the tell, and the fix re-promotes the dialog in place on `fullscreenchange`. Applied immediately rather than deferred, because the Keyguard Design tab reads `isReallyFullscreen()` and Generate Screen Openings measures the LIVE layout, both from inside Settings. (2) **"Use the whole screen" is no longer offered on WebKit** — measured on Ken's iPad (iPadOS 26.6): nothing to hide in a Home Screen app (fullscreen does not displace the iOS status bar) and it breaks Settings and the Listen button in a Safari tab, where the only control that could turn it off is the one that stops working. The *request* is gated, not just the checkbox, so a stored `true` goes inert. **Rule 19's "ONE path for every platform" ambition did not survive the device: the capability check PASSED and the feature was still useless in one mode and destructive in the other.** (3) The generation prompt never said its output would be **SPOKEN ALOUD** — the Practice-partner prompt had always said so, which is the wrong way round — so a PREFERRED card read "I fw it" (texting shorthand, unsayable by a synthesizer). Speakability rule added, deliberately **not** a register rule. (4) Separately, **no vulgarity**, absolute for now; the eventual control is **per-identified-partner** and is blocked on guardian approval. The load-bearing half is the refusal to *infer* permission — the offending turn had `partner: null` and an age in the profile, and an age with no instruction attached is worse than no age at all. (5) Deepgram: the Listen button no longer reports "listening" before the socket opens (violating a contract `stt.js` documents), `ws.onclose` now carries the close code and reason, and the **Test button opens the socket the app actually opens** — it had been connecting with `?model=` alone, so it could report a working key while listening failed, sending the diagnosis in the wrong direction. |
| 0.6.4   | 1     | **"Use the whole screen" reversed to default OFF, and the keyguard title-bar field made self-managing (Ken, August 3 2026).** (1) The setting shipped ON one release earlier; Ken lived with it and reversed it, and the reasoning is the durable part: **fullscreen cannot keep a title bar** (the API is all-or-nothing), **maximising the window is the better deal for most people** — it costs only the title bar's height, keeps the close button, and with the taskbar auto-hidden already reaches the screen edges — and **starting OFF teaches the control as an expand/contract toggle** rather than a state the user arrives in and must find their way out of. Explicit 0.6.3 choices are respected either way; only "never touched it" changes meaning. (2) **`generateScreenOpenings` now takes the title-bar offset from `isReallyFullscreen()`, not from the input field**, which is disabled with a live status while fullscreen is in effect. The real reason is not a stale value (the field has no storage accessor and resets to 0 every load — an overstatement I made and corrected): it is that **the openings are measured LIVE**, so a file is only ever valid for the mode the app is in, and a title-bar height entered while fullscreen would emit fullscreen positions plus a phantom bar. The predicate reads `document.fullscreenElement`, never the setting, since the two come apart when a request is refused or Esc is pressed. (3) **Docs**: all four ⚠ documents synced for 0.6.2–0.6.3 (My Places through both overviews and both manuals; a `<add a description>` placeholder in the Express Panel table filled; the circular privacy phrasing corrected in both overviews), the Windows manual gained the **taskbar auto-hide** step that makes a full-screen capture match the size the openings file quotes, the **Marc Delgado persona** gained a My Places section with deliberately heterogeneous fact keys, and the **demo script** now uses Marc as its user throughout. |
| 0.6.3   | 1     | **My Places, plus a fullscreen setting and three layout fixes (Ken, August 3 2026).** (1) **My Places** — a new `places.js` model (own `places.json` + cache, file-in-folder-wins), where a place is a name plus **arbitrary named facts**: no fixed field set, because a coffee shop, a clinic and a cousin's house share almost nothing, so "Location" is itself just a fact. Facts are an ordered array, so the user controls their order and duplicate keys survive. Two blocks split by what changes when — `buildBlock()` lists every place as standing context, `buildHereBlock()` names the tapped place and repeats its facts. A new Express Panel **place** toggle (olive) makes this **Phase-2 situational awareness without GPS**: the user taps where they are. Turns are stamped with the place; unlike Partner and Feeling it **persists across End conversation** (Ken — ending a conversation does not move you). (2) **"Use the whole screen"** (Settings → General, default **on**) — Fullscreen API on the Start tap, capability-gated so one path serves every platform, with a `display_override` manifest hint. Measured: it moves every keyguard hole, which is why it is a setting. (3) **Privacy wording corrected in all three models** — the rule ended "only include them if the user's chosen response requires it", which names nothing that exists when the options are written; it now names the two real prompts (the partner asks, or the user steers via Reframe), guarded by tests. A stale A5 module note describing pre-v0.3.12 behavior was fixed too, and both User Manuals corrected in place. (4) **Express Panel settings tab no longer scrolls horizontally** — the cause was Rule 15 reaching the editor's swatches and icon buttons (99.6px each where the design says 1.7rem/2rem), not the phrase field, which could already shrink to zero. (5) **A Settings tab whose controls would overlap drops to a single column** (Rule 18) — CSS multi-column paints a too-wide item over the next column and reports nothing. (6) **Hover no longer sticks on a touch screen** (Rule 20) — 44 `:hover` rules had no `@media (hover: hover)` guard, so a tapped button kept its dark state; the thirteen conversation-surface rules are now gated, `:focus-visible` left global. (7) **A tapped choice chip shows as chosen** (Rule 21) — the steering was already re-sent by "New N", but nothing on screen said so. |
| 0.6.1   | 1     | **Settings decluttered, and a mojibake bug repaired (Ken, August 1 2026).** (1) **All per-control explanatory text removed from the Settings panel** — 38 hint paragraphs from `index.html` plus the per-control help generated in `app.js`, `control-phrases-editor.js` and `express-editor.js`. Ken: *"way too much explanatory (hint) text… remove all text associated with individual controls."* A tooltip is not the alternative — it needs hover and this app targets direct select — so the text moved to the manuals, staged verbatim in `SETTINGS-HELP-TEXT.md`. Tab-level intros, live status regions, count spans and empty-state messages were kept, because none of them is explanation. Recorded as **UI Layout Rule 14**. (2) **Mojibake repaired in `app.js` and `sw.js`** — em dashes, curly quotes, bullets and box-drawing characters were rendering as double-encoded sequences in Settings on both platforms, including the redacted API key. Cause: a past version bump rewrote exactly those two files through PowerShell `Get-Content`/`Set-Content`, which on PS 5.1 decodes UTF-8 as Windows-1252 and adds a BOM. Cosmetic only — `keyFieldValue()` guards on a dataset flag, not the placeholder string, so no key could ever be overwritten. `RELEASING.md` now warns both bump steps off that path with a copy-pasteable check. |
| 0.5.94  | 1     | **API-key feedback: missing-key prompt, format warning, live Test button (Ken, July 17 2026).** Ken (testing the 0.5.93 deploy) had no visible cue for a missing key and no error for an incomplete paste; the User Manual §3.2 promised a red invalid-key warning that was never built, and the "No API key set" message went to the hidden status bar (v0.5.2). Built three surfaces: (1) a pre-start **"no API key" prompt** (`#apiKeyPrompt` over the transcript region, amber, with an Open Settings button; shows when no key is saved, informational — the user can still Start and use manual AAC); (2) a **format warning under the API Key field** (`llm.validateKeyFormat`, no network — flags missing `sk-ant-` prefix / spaces / too-short as you type); (3) a **"Test" button** (`llm.testApiKey` → `GET /v1/models`, bills no tokens) that reports "working" / "rejected" — the only surface that catches a subtly-truncated key. Verified in preview incl. a live 401 for a bogus key via browser CORS; 119/119 tests. Docs follow-up deferred: User Manual §3.2 wording + Test-button/prompt mention. |
| 0.5.93  | 1     | **Wind-down and closing split into two card categories; static palettes paginate (Ken, July 17 2026).** Wind-down statements ("I should get going.") and closings ("Bye!") are now distinct steps of the CA closing sequence. The **Wind down** button shows wind-down statements; selecting one auto-offers the goodbyes (`engine.showClosings()`); a partner-initiated close (AI `CLOSING` or the `looksLikeClosing` fast-path) also shows the goodbyes. Re-pressing **Wind down** re-offers wind-downs — no escalation, just "a different 4": openers / wind-downs / closings now **paginate**, and both the **"New N"** button and a Wind-down re-press dip to the next page of the defined set (wrapping, no LLM). New `SLOT.WIND_DOWN`; `control-phrases.js` `closers` → `windDowns` + `closings` (defaults + seeded-watermark; a legacy single-`closers` file reseeds both from defaults — single-user pre-beta, re-edit via Controls). Controls editor gains two sections (Wind-down statements / Closings). `app.js`: `renderStaticPalette`/`pageWindow`/`staticOffsets` + `offerClosings`; the Wind down / Initiate / Regenerate / CLOSING paths route through the pager, cleared to regenerate when AI cards show. 119/119 unit tests; static flows + Controls-tab editing verified in preview. |
| 0.5.92  | 1     | **Pardon re-speak becomes a separate turn; space between speech segments (Ken, July 13 2026).** Two issues from a pardon-flow transcript. (1) After **Ask them to repeat**, the partner's re-speak was appended to their earlier turn (and rendered before the pardon): "…great weekend.Yeah, I had a great weekend too." Refines v0.5.87 ("repeat appends to it") — the re-speak should be a NEW turn AFTER the pardon, without discarding the original. Fix (`handlePardon`): after `logSpokenUserTurn` commits the kept turn + the pardon, `finalizePendingPartnerTurn()` (detach + finalize storage, cleaned=raw) + `stt.resetTranscript()` + clear `currentPartnerText`/`pendingPartnerHistoryIdx`, so the next partner speech is a fresh turn; engine repair resolution unaffected. (2) The recognizer returns segments with no separating space, so "Good morning." + "How was your weekend?" concatenated into "…weekend.How…". Fix (`stt.js`): a `joinParts()` helper joins finalized segments (and the interim) with single spaces everywhere `accumulatedText` / `getCurrentTranscript` / the checkpoint text / `dropLastStatement` are built. Also recorded that transcript/pane fidelity is foundational for the future relive-and-critique (Phase-3 conversation-review) feature, not just test hygiene. Unit-tested (spaced concat + checkpoint); 116/116. Boots clean; the live pardon flow is the on-device retest. |
| 0.5.91  | 1     | **Abort placeholders the instant a speaking button is pressed (Ken, July 13 2026).** On-device: **Repeat what I said** was cut off partway by a placeholder. Cause: Say again / Hold on did NOT invalidate an in-flight `generateOptions` (a response selection does), so when the partner's already-running LLM round-trip completed it called `placeholders.start()` again; with the initial-delay clock measured from the partner's pause (already elapsed) the placeholder fired immediately and its `tts.speak` cancelled the button's speech. Fix, two layers: (1) a `placeholderEpoch` (app.js) bumped by `abortPlaceholders()` — Say again / Hold on call it, and `generateOptions` captures the epoch and skips `placeholders.start()` if it changed mid-generation, suppressing the placeholder WITHOUT discarding the response options (separate from `generationToken`, which a response selection uses to cancel generation outright); Wind down uses `generationToken++`. (2) A hard backstop in `placeholders.js` — `setUserSpeakingGate(() => speakingUserStatement)` so `speakNext` defers (never barges) while the app speaks the user's own statement. Buttons that already `generationToken++` (response / Ask-them-to-repeat / Express / composer) were already covered. Unit-tested (the gate defers then speaks once the user is done); 115/115. Boots clean; acoustic timing is the on-device retest. |
| 0.5.90  | 1     | **Harden TTS-echo excision — partial / mis-heard / lagged echo (Ken, July 13 2026).** On-device: after **Repeat what I said**, the mic caught only the TAIL of the re-spoken response ("had some time to relax") and appended it to the partner's turn; a placeholder "Still mulling that over" came back mis-heard as "Steel mulling that over." Ken's directive: keep the mic hot (talk-over stays) — excise the app's own phrases from the STT stream more robustly instead. Fix entirely in `stt.js` `isEcho`: (1) `fuzzySlice()` matches an arbitrary CONTIGUOUS token slice of what we said (either direction), catching a suffix/middle fragment the old exact/prefix/embedded matcher missed; (2) `tokensSimilar()` allows a small per-token edit distance (2 for len≥4, 1 for len 3; short words exact) so "still"→"steel" still matches; (3) decoupled windows — `ECHO_TAIL_MS`=1500 still gates the checkpoint (a genuine reply isn't delayed) but a new `ECHO_MATCH_MS`=4000 keeps a spoken phrase matchable for excision long enough to catch the cloud recognizer's late echo (past the tail). Accepted tradeoff: within the match window a partner utterance that genuinely repeats our phrase is treated as echo. Unit-tested (suffix slice, still→steel, late echo past the tail, no-over-drop); 114/114. Boots clean; the acoustic loop + cloud timing is the on-device retest boundary. Robust long-term answer remains Phase-2 partner voice recognition. |
| 0.5.89  | 1     | **Fix conversation-pane ordering — spoken-command turn jumped above the partner (Ken, July 13 2026).** On-device follow-up to 0.5.88: after the partner replied, tapping **Repeat what I said** (or Hold on / Ask them to repeat) rendered the user's line ABOVE the partner's reply in the pane, though the transcript had it correctly after. Cause: the pane showed the partner's in-progress turn as a bottom-pinned live line (`ui.setLiveTranscript`) while a `logSpokenUserTurn` command turn was appended into the committed history above it. Fix (app.js, pane-only — storage/transcript were already correct): `flushLivePartnerToHistory()` promotes the live partner turn into `conversationHistory` (tracked by `pendingPartnerHistoryIdx`) before appending the command turn; `commitExchange`/repair use `placePartnerTurn` (update the promoted entry in place, keeping it before the command, else append); `updatePartnerLive` keeps a promoted turn updating in place instead of re-showing the live line; `logSpokenUserTurn` also writes the partner's heard text to the transcript first (pane+transcript identical even before the partner's first pause). Traced through partner1→user1→partner2→say-again→response (both order partner2 before the say-again); boots clean; live-mic ordering is the on-device retest boundary. 110/110 tests. |
| 0.5.88  | 1     | **Transcript mirrors the conversation pane — incremental partner writes (Ken, July 13 2026).** The recorded transcript (`conversations/<id>.json`) now mirrors the on-screen conversation pane at all times, so it's a reliable record while the app misbehaves during testing (kept until/unless it causes performance issues). Reverses the prior commit-at-response model. Terminology fixed: "the transcript" = the JSON file, "the conversation pane" = the UI element. Behavior: the file is **created on Listen/Start** (`startConversationLog` made idempotent), the partner's **raw line is written at each pause** (`logPartnerInterim`) and a **continuation overwrites it + clears the cleaned line** (no duplicate turn), and on a response the **user turn is written immediately** (no longer waiting on the AI cleanup round-trip) while the partner entry is **finalized in place** (cleaned line filled) in the background — partner-then-user order preserved; interruption/repair/End-Start finalize the pending entry instead of appending a duplicate. New pure module `transcript-log.js` (`upsertPartnerInterim`/`finalizePartner`, the array-shaping rules) unit-tested (`tests/transcript-log.test.mjs`, 5 cases); `storage.js` tracks `pendingPartnerTurn` and exposes `logPartnerInterim`/`detachPendingPartnerTurn`/`finalizePartnerTurn` (old `logPartnerSpeech` removed). Verified end-to-end through the real `storage.js` against an in-memory FSA folder mock (file-on-start, raw→overwrite-clearing-cleaned, user-immediate, finalize-in-place); 110/110 tests; on-device confirmation is the usual granted-folder boundary. Manual test plan §8 rewritten + terminology swept. |
| 0.5.87  | 1     | **STT interim preservation + settings-profile picker fix + "Ask them to repeat" keeps the transcript (Ken, July 12 2026).** (1) Browser recognition ends continuous capture periodically and the app restarts it; the restart cleared `currentInterim`, and the fresh session doesn't re-hear already-spoken audio, so not-yet-finalized interim words shown live were lost — interrupting the partner mid-utterance recorded only the finalized segment ("I was"). Fix: `stt.js` `onend` restart branch flushes `currentInterim` into `accumulatedText`/`segments` before clearing (regression test added; no duplication — the new session only transcribes forward). (2) Settings-profile picker showed the wrong name after save/load/restart (the data always applied — proven via an in-memory-folder round-trip test): added a machine-local `activeSettingsProfile` pointer (in `PROFILE_EXCLUDE`), set on save/apply and cleared on delete-of-active; `renderSettingsProfiles` selects it + shows "In use: …". (3) Enter in the profile-name box saves the current settings (`profileNameInput.onkeydown`; the on-screen keyboard's Enter dispatches the same keydown). (4) "Ask them to repeat" (`handlePardon`) no longer discards the partner's transcript — removed `stt.dropLastStatement()`, keeps `heardPartnerText()` so the re-speak appends (reverses v0.5.32). (5) Controls-tab label "'Pardon?' phrase" → "'Ask them to repeat' phrase" (`control-phrases-editor.js`). Verified: 105/105 unit tests, clean boot, label + Enter handler confirmed in the running app. |
| 0.5.86  | 1     | **End/Start now commit the partner's pending turn instead of dropping it (Ken, July 12 2026).** Diagnosed from two attached conversation logs: a partner spoke but the user ended before replying, so (a) the uncommitted partner turn was dropped, and (b) it leaked into the NEXT conversation — `terminateConversation()` cleared `currentPartnerText` but not the STT buffer (`stopListening()` keeps `accumulatedText`), so `heardPartnerText()` read the stale text when the user picked the next opener and it was committed at the top of the new conversation though nobody spoke. Fix: `terminateConversation()` (shared by End + Start) captures the pending partner turn + situation stamp up front, `stt.resetTranscript()`s the buffer, does the instant UI teardown, then — before the id reset and before re-seeding the next conversation's privacy — writes the pending turn RAW (no AI cleanup round-trip, so the flush can't race the id reset) to the CURRENT `<id>.json` via `logPartnerSpeech`, then `resetConversationId()`. Made `terminateConversation` async and `await`ed in both callers (`handleInitiate`, `handleEndConversation`). Verified in preview: boots clean, Start renders openers / End clears them through the async paths, no console errors; the disk-write flush needs a live mic + granted folder (tablet). |
| 0.5.85  | 1     | **Settings keyboard-navigation + Express per-item Save + layout rename (Ken, July 11 2026).** Feedback batch on the on-screen keyboard / tab order. (1) **Settings tabs are keyboard-navigable** via a roving-tabindex ARIA tablist (`aria-orientation="vertical"`): the tab column is a single Tab stop, and **↑/↓** (plus Home/End) move between tabs while Tab continues into the panel — so a physical-keyboard user reaches About Me from General without tabbing through nine tabs' content (`initSettingsTabs`/new `activateSettingsTab` in `app.js`; `.settings-tab:focus-visible` ring; `openSettings` keeps the roving state in sync). ←/→ are inert (vertical strip). (2) **On-screen keyboard keys removed from the Tab order** unconditionally — every `.kbd-key` (and the hidden prediction buttons) gets `tabIndex = -1` in `keyboard.js` `renderRows`/`build`, since keys are pointer targets, never Tab targets, in both typing modes. (3) **Per-item "Save" button on the Express Panel editor** (`express-editor.js` `buildRow`): commits the item and calls `keyboard.hideKeyboard()` so an on-screen-keyboard user can put the keyboard away and see the panel/list again (the Hide button having been removed in v0.5.38); green text button, `.ee-save` CSS overrides the 2rem icon sizing. (4) **Layout names "Keyboard" → "Layout"** in `keyboard-layouts.js` (Side Layout 1–10, Bottom Layout 1–11) — they set both the keyboard and the Express Panel grid. (5) Profile-load confirm button relabeled **"Load & reload" → "Load"** (`app.js`). Item 3 from the feedback (a "template" caption for physical mode) was dropped — physical mode already shows only the Express Panel, no keyboard. Verified in the running app (DOM assertions + reload): roving tabindex, ↑/↓ nav with ←/→ inert, all 33 keys `tabindex=-1`, 32 Express rows each with a Save button, layout selects renamed; no console errors. |
| 0.5.84  | 1     | **Wind-down card count fix + composer Tab trap + keyguard-tab separation settings + fast-path goodbyes (Ken, July 11 2026).** (1) A partner's reply to a wind-down came back as the FULL closer list and rendered UNCAPPED (`ui.showResponses` non-responding branch keyed `perCell` off item count), so 8 closings showed even in 1-card mode; now the branch caps/stacks by the `responses per category` setting — ≤4 items are one-per-cell (repair-of-self stays 3 single cards), >4 caps to 4 (1-card) or stacks to 8 (8-card). (2) The "In my own words" composer is a soft modal (base UI intentionally live), so a physical-keyboard Tab leaked focus into the base controls behind it; added a Tab/Shift+Tab focus trap (`composerTabTrap` in `ui.js`) cycling textarea → Speak → Reframe → Cancel. (3) Moved "Keyboard separation" from Speech & Input to the **Keyguard Design** tab and added a new **"Transcript separation"** slider there (shortens the transcript to open a gap above the command bar; command bar + dock holes stay put) — new `transcriptSepPos` storage + `--transcript-sep` var, applied in both docks. (4) **Fast-path closing:** while winding down (PRE_CLOSING), a plain-farewell partner reply re-offers the goodbyes IMMEDIATELY without an AI round-trip (so the user can speak another closing sooner) — `conversation-logic.looksLikeClosing()` (precision-biased keyword+length heuristic, documented field-feedback note) gates `generateOptions`' early path, which feeds the engine a synthetic CLOSING classification; a non-match falls through to the normal AI path. Verified in preview (both docks, closing-count matrix, tab-trap wrap, transcript-sep in side+bottom, synthetic-CLOSING integration); 104/104 unit tests (+3 `looksLikeClosing`). |
| 0.5.83  | 1     | **Named settings profiles + About Me becomes an ordinary Settings tab (Ken, July 10 2026).** (1) **Settings profiles** (Settings → General, below Data Folder): save the whole `aac_settings` bundle to `settings/<name>.json` in the data folder and re-apply/delete it in one tap — a repeatable on-device test baseline and the seed of cross-device transfer / the PRE-BETA data-folder-settings work. New `storage.js` `saveSettingsProfile`/`listSettingsProfiles`/`settingsProfileExists`/`applySettingsProfile`/`deleteSettingsProfile`; Load writes localStorage then `location.reload()` so every setting re-applies exactly as at startup. **Excludes** the API key (SEC-6 — never written to a possibly cloud-synced folder), the token-usage counters, and `lastSeenVersion`; applying a profile preserves those machine-local keys. Danger-confirmed on save-over/load/delete; the name field is in the on-screen-keyboard scope. Verified end-to-end against an in-memory folder mock (round-trip, key-exclusion, key-preservation, case-insensitive overwrite, delete). (2) **About Me** converted from a full-screen `#worldviewScreen` overlay (dark title bar + "Done" button, launched by closing Settings) to an **ordinary inline Settings tab** rendered into `.tab-panel[data-tab="aboutme"]` — no title bar, no Done, dismissed by the shared **Close** button like every other tab; sub-pages (modules / People) show an in-content `.wv-page-title` heading instead. Removed the overlay lifecycle (show/hide/`setBackgroundInert`/`close`) from `worldview-ui.js` (the Settings modal already inerts the background and hosts the keyboard); the tab handler calls `worldviewUI.open()` to render, and `handleSettingsTab` hides the keyboard when leaving any non-keyboard tab. Removed the `#worldviewScreen`/`.worldview-header` styles + its dock-reservation rules (the Settings dialog already reserves the dock). Also carries the pending "goodbye no longer repeats the partner's last words" fix. |
| 0.5.82  | 1     | **Stop gating generation on turn_status — the user judges turn completion (Ken, July 10 2026; reverses design §8).** After the 0.5.81 fix, a *later* short/disfluent go-ahead ("Go ahead. Uh, my — my ears are wide open.") could still stall silently, because the false-TRP guard has no recovery when the model misreads a complete turn as incomplete. Ken's call: stop trying to detect when the partner is "done" (fragile from garbled STT, hard even for humans). New model: every silence checkpoint generates AND shows a palette, refined on each later pause; the partner's turn is complete only when the USER responds or ends the conversation. Removed design §8's false-TRP suppression guard (and the interim pause-to-complete fallback). CA-checked: doesn't violate CA — turn-completion is a participant judgment, and the device only *prepares* options (never auto-acts), so there's no false-TRP action. Kept `partner_action` + `is_repair_initiator`; `turn_status` is informational only. Engine now updates the same ongoing partner FPP in place per pause (no duplicate). Placeholders unchanged (fire after the pause, abort on partner resume). Known limitation accepted: resumption *during* an active placeholder isn't detected until it finishes (echo guard) — Phase-2 partner voice ID is the fix. Also created the tester-facing `Conversant AAC Known Issues.docx`. |
| 0.5.81  | 1     | **Fix silent stall in user-started conversations (Ken, July 10 2026).** When the user opened with a starter and the partner replied with a short go-ahead ("Yeah sure, any time you want, just let me know"), the app produced no placeholder and no response options — a silent stall, nothing logged. Root cause: in the user-led case the model misclassified the short go-ahead as a non-COMPLETE turn, and the engine's false-TRP guard then suppressed the whole lead palette. Fix: the "User is leading" generation prompt now requires `turn_status: COMPLETE` + the four lead responses for a go-ahead, and the engine skips the false-TRP suppression when the partner is replying to a user-opened sequence (normalizing the status to COMPLETE). Added silent-dead-end instrumentation — any "complete turn but no options" / "non-COMPLETE while user leading" outcome now logs (transcript red-wash + errors.log) so this class of failure can never be invisible again; the loop-decision logic + tripwires were extracted to `conversation-logic.js`. Also added a three-tier `node --test` harness (`tests/`, ~95 deterministic tests + live-API scenarios) and an on-device manual test plan with seed fixtures. Confirmed against the live model. |
| 0.5.80  | 1     | **Fix the composer's leftover clipped-looking border (Ken, July 9 2026).** The "In my own words" container drew a 2px inset framing border whose rounded corner clipped at the response-footprint edges, reading as cut off. Removed (`box-shadow: none`) — a flush, borderless container also matches the Response Palette it overlays. |
| 0.5.79  | 1     | **Graceful behavior when the AI is unreachable + composer polish (Ken, July 9 2026).** When generation fails (API/network down), the partner's raw words stay visible in the transcript styled blue/italic ("uncleaned" — the AI can't tidy them), the red-wash heads-up stays, and the user replies via the Express Panel / "In my own words" (committed + saved as before) — nothing is committed on failure, so words aren't duplicated or lost if the partner keeps talking. A partner turn recorded without the AI cleanup pass (this case or an interruption) is flagged `uncleaned` and rendered blue/italic. STT errors are now logged (tripping the red wash) and the recognition restart-loop is stopped on a surfaced (e.g. `network`) error, since browser STT is cloud-based and can't transcribe with no internet. Composer: removed the "In my own words" input's border AND its focus ring (edgeless in every state — the whole box is the predicted-word tap target); Cancel now has the same dark border as Reframe. (7) **Errors interleave into the conversation JSON** in time order (`storage.logErrorToConversation`, flushed immediately). | 0.5.75  | 1     | **Transcript error indicator — faint-red wash on any logged error (Ken, July 8 2026).** A non-verbal heads-up that the app hit an error mid-conversation (a hiccup the user/partner should expect and pass to support) — no worded message on screen; details still go to the error log. Wired at the single choke point `storage.logError`, so it trips on **thrown** API/parse failures AND the **silent** no-usable-responses paths (empty/malformed `responses[]`, INCOMPLETE classification, superseded generation token) that never throw. `logError` dispatches an `aac-error-logged` event (keeps storage decoupled from the UI); `app.js` listens → `ui.setTranscriptError(true)`; `ui.setTranscriptError` toggles `has-error` on `#transcript`; `showResponses` clears it (sticky-until-next-success), as does conversation reset (Start/End). `.transcript-log.has-error` = faint red bg `rgba(220,53,69,0.08)` + red border, 0.25s fade. The old worded "amber box" (`showResponseError`) left in place for now; CLAUDE.md TODO tracks deciding its disposition before public release. Verified in preview: boots clean; the event trips `has-error` on `#transcript` and the rule computes to the faint-red bg + red border; removing the class restores transparent/grey. |
| 0.5.73  | 1     | **Error log grouped by conversation + Copy bundles the transcript (Ken, July 7 2026).** Follow-up to v0.5.72. `renderErrorLog()` now groups entries by `conversation` (newest conversation first, `(none)` last) instead of a flat chronological list, via `groupErrorsByConversation()`. The Copy button no longer copies the raw textarea — `buildErrorReport()` assembles a full report: for each conversation with errors, its transcript followed by that conversation's errors. The transcript comes from new `storage.readConversationLog(id)` (reads `conversations/<id>.json` back), or, when the id is the still-open conversation (`storage.getConversationId()`), from the live in-memory `conversationHistory` (covers an error that fired before the turn was committed to disk); otherwise "[not available — no data folder / not saved]". So a pasted bug report carries the conversation, not just the error line. Verified in preview: grouping (2 conversations newest-first), the report structure (header + per-conversation transcript/errors), Copy → "Copied ✓"; on-disk transcript needs a granted FSA folder to exercise. |
| 0.5.72  | 1     | **Persistent error log correlated to the conversation (Ken, July 7 2026).** Errors (API 429/529 failures, JSON parse failures, transcript cleanup, repair/reframe/regenerate) previously went only to the now-hidden status bar + console, so an intermittent live-demo failure — most importantly "no response options" — left no trace. New `storage.logError(context, message, extra)` writes each error to a capped (200) localStorage ring buffer AND appends it to `errors.log` in the data folder (FSA `createWritable({keepExistingData:true})` + seek-to-end append), stamped with the app version and a per-conversation id. That id comes from `ensureConversationId()` and is SHARED with the conversation log filename (`<id>.json`) — reset in `terminateConversation` via `storage.resetConversationId()` — so an error matches its conversation (both derive from the same id; second-resolution, same as the existing log filename). Viewer in Settings → About (`#errorLogView` textarea + count + Copy + Clear-with-`confirmDanger`); `renderErrorLog()` populated on Settings open. Catch sites wired: `generateOptions`/`regenerate`/`reframe`/`repairSelf`/`cleanupTranscript`. Generation failures also now surface VISIBLY in the response region via new `ui.showResponseError(msg, onRetry)` (amber dashed box + "Try again"), replacing the blank look. Verified in preview: viewer renders seeded entries with conversation id; logError stamps version + shared/stable-within-conversation id + resets; showResponseError renders + retry fires; Clear opens the danger dialog; no console errors (the data-folder append needs a granted FSA folder to exercise, same path as conversation logging). **Root-cause still open:** the log is the instrument to confirm whether the demo "no options" is API 429/529 (no retry yet) — automatic retry-with-backoff is the likely follow-up fix. |
| 0.5.71  | 1     | **Word-prediction acceptance: tap the field, not a separator (Ken, July 7 2026).** The inline ghost completion is no longer accepted by a word-separator — space / comma / period / Enter now insert literally and dismiss the ghost (`handleKey` in keyboard.js: `learnCurrentWord()` + `clearGhost()` instead of `acceptGhost()`). This fixes the silent, invisible swap where "Yes" + space became "Yesterday" (the user may not even be looking at the box). Acceptance is now an explicit **tap anywhere in the active field** — a new document `pointerdown` handler: when `ghostWord` is showing and the tap's `closest(IN_SCOPE)` is the `activeField`, it `preventDefault()`s (keeps focus, caret stays at the end) and `acceptGhost()`s. The whole field is one big FIXED acceptance target, so there's no moving inline tap target to chase (keyguard-safe) and no silent separator acceptance; the `#predGhost` overlay is `pointer-events:none` so the tap lands on the field. Applies to every predicted field (composer + About Me), so prediction stays acceptable everywhere now that separators don't accept; the only trade-off (Ken's call — "this region won't be used as a typical composition space") is that a tap while a ghost is showing no longer positions the caret. Verified in preview via dispatched `pointerdown` (accepts, `defaultPrevented`) + all separator cases (space/comma/period/Enter insert literally); real trusted-tap needs the device (the preview click tool doesn't drive pointer events through the overlay). No console errors. |

(1) **Per-conversation privacy — "Don't save this conversation."** New Command Bar toggle `#privacyBtn` (shield-with-slash `noSave` icon; amber `.private-on` selected state, Rule 6) + a Settings → Conversation default checkbox `#noSaveDefaultInput`. When active the conversation is NOT written to the data folder: `storage.setConversationSaving(false)` makes `startConversationLog`/`logPartnerSpeech`/`logUserResponse` no-op entirely (no file, no turns). Runtime flag `conversationPrivate` seeds from the Settings default (`loadNoSaveDefault`) and re-seeds at every Start/End conversation (`terminateConversation`) so a per-conversation choice never carries over; the button toggles it live. (2) **About Me tab moved below General** in the Settings nav (order: General · About Me · Text Size · Speech & Input · …). (3) **Response-card text size** setting (`responseFontSelect` → `--response-font-scale`, applied to `.response-text`), joining the existing Transcript/Composer/Express scales. (4) **New "Text Size" tab** — all four size selects (Response cards, Transcript, "In my own words", Express Panel) moved off the over-long Speech & Input tab onto their own short tab (Ken chose this over reorganizing in place). (2) `--grid-gap` extended beyond the dock to the conversation main area (region gaps + outer padding), the response area / palette / stacked-card gaps, the Command Bar, and the overlay **surface paddings** (`#settingsContent`, `.dialog-actions`, `#worldviewContent`). **One technical exception (Ken acknowledged the risk):** the on-screen-keyboard **keys** are sized BY the dock band (itself derived from `--btn-min-dim`), so a hard min there would overflow the fixed dock — they scale through the dock, not a min. **Boundary I drew (flag to Ken):** fine typographic spacing inside Settings/About Me (e.g. `.setting-group` margins, chip-row gaps) was left as-is — squeezing those to the gap value hurts readability with no keyguard benefit (those overlays are the keyguard's "open window", not under it). So **button size is authoritative** (sets the actual key width in a side dock / key height in a bottom dock) and raising it grows the dock along its expandable axis — side dock **widens** (Settings/main area shrinks + reflows), bottom dock grows **taller** (transcript shrinks). | 0.5.42  | 1     | **Inline word prediction — ghost completion, accept on any separator (Ken, June 29 2026).** Word prediction returns to the UI as an **inline ghost** instead of a button row (which had no keyguard-safe home): as the user types on the on-screen keyboard, the single best completion appears **inline, right after their text**, drawn **bold** in a tunable color (`--pred-ghost-color`, default a high-contrast blue) on a subtle tint (`--pred-ghost-bg`) — bold + tint mark it provisional WITHOUT relying on low contrast (legible for low vision; bold also relaxes WCAG to 3:1). **Accept by typing any word-separator** (space / comma / period / `? ! ; :` / Enter / etc. — anything outside the predictor's `[A-Za-z']` word set): the separator completes the ghost word, then is itself inserted (space→space, comma→`,`), so there's **no moving tap target** (keyguard-safe, no contenteditable). Implemented in `keyboard.js` as a **non-interactive mirror overlay** (`#predGhost`): it copies the field's font/padding/border and renders the typed text transparent (reserving exact width) + the styled suffix, positioned over the field so the real text shows through as one line; shown only when the **caret is at the end** of the value (true appending) so nothing follows the ghost. Scope: all on-screen-keyboard fields except the API key; capitalization matches the typed prefix; the old hidden prediction-button infrastructure (`prediction.js`, learning on space/enter) is reused. Verified in preview: "Th"→bold "e" (case-matched "The"); space accepts → "The "; "ca"+comma → "The can,"; ghost box aligns to the field exactly; mirrored text transparent; clears on blur/field-end; no console errors. *Single-line inputs that overflow horizontally can clip the ghost past the right edge (accepted limitation); the composer textarea wraps so it's fine. | 0.5.27  | 1     | **Keyboard toolbar ↔ Express Panel control-row alignment** (Ken). The keyboard's Cut/Copy/Paste/Hide toolbar (`.kbd-toolbar`, was `min-height: 2.4rem`) was shorter than the Express Panel's four override buttons (`#epControls`/`.persist-btn`, ~44px from `0.7rem` vertical padding), so the keyboard's keys sat higher than the phrase grid and the two dock layouts didn't overlay hole-for-hole. Both control rows are now pinned to ONE shared height and the grids beneath start at the same y in **both** docks (verified bottom: rows at y=420; side: y=51). Introduces the **`--btn-min-dim`** token (root, default 2.75rem) = "minimum button dimension," the single seam a future Settings control will raise to enlarge targets fleet-wide; here its first job is alignment. Both `#epControls` and `.kbd-toolbar` use `flex: 0 0 var(--btn-min-dim)`; `.kbd-tool`/`.kbd-pred-btn`/`.persist-btn` floor their `min-height` to it; `.persist-btn` vertical padding removed (height now comes from row stretch, not padding, so it can't grow past the keyboard's toolbar). Content buttons (keys/phrase cards) deliberately NOT floored to the token yet — the bottom dock is a fixed `48vh` band of ~12 stretched rows, so a hard floor there would overflow; growing content buttons is the future Settings job that must reconcile with the fixed dock. |
| 0.5.28  | 1     | **Symbols page now matches the active letters layout's geometry** (Ken — Spatial Stability). The 123 (symbols/numbers) page was ONE fixed grid per dock (5-wide side, 10-wide bottom), so when the selected letters layout was a different shape (e.g. the 3-wide `S6`), tapping 123 changed the whole grid and a single static keyguard's holes no longer lined up. Replaced the static `SYMBOLS` object with `buildSymbolsPage(letterRows)` in `keyboard-layouts.js`: it GENERATES the symbols page from whichever letters layout is active — identical rows, identical spans, action/space/blank/pred cells left exactly in place, only each LETTER cell swapped for a symbol (from an ordered `SYMBOL_POOL`: digits → common specials → rarer, surplus letter cells go blank if the pool runs out) and the 123 key relabeled to ABC. `keyboard.js` `currentRows()` calls it for the active layout. Geometry is therefore guaranteed congruent between the two pages for every layout — verified all 21 (S1–S10, B1–B11) congruent letters↔symbols. |

(1) **Bug:** opening with a pre-question ("Can I ask you something?") then the partner's "sure" produced options as if the *partner* had asked the opener. Fix in `engine.js`: selecting an OPENER now pushes a **USER-opened** sequence (floor → partner); when the partner's COMPLETE reply lands and the innermost open sequence is USER-opened (non-repair), it's an **SPP from the partner** — pop it, push no partner FPP, floor → user to LEAD. `buildRequestContext` exposes `user_holds_floor_to_lead`; `llm.js` prompt tells the model to generate *lead/continue* moves (treat "sure"/"go ahead" as a go-ahead, not a question). (2) **UI cleanup:** removed the "Conversation" label, the engine **mode chip**, and the **Engine diagnostics** `<details>` (kept the guarded JS — a new launch path TBD if needed later); **Start/End conversation** widened to flex-fill their row like the Say-again/Hold-on persist controls (room for more buttons later); move cards **dropped the category badge + latency dot** (color + position carry category; all cards spoken instantly). (3) **Nicknames:** `relationships.buildBlock()` now instructs the model to address people by their preferred term ("mom", not "Mary") when a nickname is set. (4) **QWERTY:** new bottom layout **B11** "QWERTY (touch-typist, 3-row)" — letters in QWERTY order, grid-aligned (q/a/z share column 1), space on the bottom row, specials on the shared symbols page; selectable in Settings. Resolves the long-deferred "should composed utterances push to history?" question — yes. |
| 0.3.18  | 1     | Anti-fabrication guardrail in the generation prompt (Ken, June 20 2026): the AI was inventing specific autobiographical events — e.g. "I played a game with Tyler last night and beat him!" — because the prompt said "speak as the user / the most likely thing THIS user would say" with no constraint against making up episodes. Added a top-priority "speak only to what is real" rule: never invent specific events/outcomes/scores/dates/places/names not given; MAY use standing profile facts (habitual activities, interests, people) and general/non-committal replies; keep answers GENERAL when a specific detail is unknown ("Been playing online games with friends lately") rather than fabricating particulars. Also constrained the DISPREFERRED slot's "account/reason" so it can't invent a concrete excuse (no fake "dentist appointment at 3"). First concrete implementation of the conversational-honesty principle (Further Design Thoughts #2). |
| 0.3.17  | 1     | People editor scroll: editing a person below the fold no longer moves the page on Save/Cancel either — the list scroll position is captured when Edit is pressed and restored when the edit form closes, so the page returns exactly where it was (heights match: the form becomes a card again). Fresh entry from the home screen still lands at the top. |
| 0.3.16  | 1     | "Show me different options" regenerate control (Ken, June 19 2026): when the offered palette isn't quite right, re-runs generation for the SAME partner turn with the rejected options passed as `avoid` (llm.js), refreshing ONLY the palette via `engine.refreshPalette` (stack/mode/floor untouched — no duplicate FPP). Whole-palette regenerate; per-move regenerate + multiple-options-per-slot recorded as a to-do. Also merged the two misheard-partner controls into one: the old "Pardon?" (managed the stack but kept the garbled capture) and "Please repeat what you said." (discarded the capture but ignored the stack) each did half the job; the single **Pardon?** now does both — speaks "Sorry, I didn't catch that. Could you say it again?", discards the captured transcript, AND pushes the (deduped) repair sequence. The standalone "Please repeat" persistent control is gone; its slot now holds the regenerate control. |

## Relationship Data — its own graph model, separate from the questionnaire (decided + BUILT June 15 2026, v0.2.27)

**BUILT (June 15 2026), v0.2.27 — the relationship graph is live end-to-end.** Files/wiring:
- **`app/js/relationships.js`** — the model layer. Graph shape `{ version, updated, migratedFromWorldview, people:[{id, name, private, attrs:{about}}], edges:[{from, to, type, attrs}] }`; the user is the implicit node `"me"` and a person's relationship is the `type` of the `me→person` edge (person↔person edges are supported by the schema, not yet by the UI). API: `load`/`save`/`syncToFolder` (**same "file in the folder wins" rule as worldview v0.2.25** — its own cache key `aac_relationships`), `listPeople` (denormalized name+relationship+about+private), `addPerson`/`updatePerson`/`removePerson`, `resetAll`, `count`, `migrateFromWorldview(wv)`, and `buildBlock()`.
- **`buildBlock()`** contributes a "People in my life:" block to the LLM profile. **Private people are never named or described** — surfaced only as a phrase-around instruction by their relationship (mirrors the worldview privacy rule).
- **No migration (Ken, June 15 2026).** A first cut wired an idempotent `migrateFromWorldview()` into app boot to import the old module A3 answers; **removed** — Ken is the only user and had entered little relationship data, so he re-enters people via the People editor rather than carry migration code (and no standalone script either). The old A3 answers remain in `worldview.json` but are **orphaned** (no longer in the registry, so `worldview.buildBlock()` ignores them) — harmless dead data.
- **Registry change** (`app/data/worldview-questions.json`): **module A3 deleted**; its one genuinely-flat field `address_terms` ("what do you call people") moved into **A1 About You**. People/pets are now the graph, not questionnaire fields.
- **UI** (`worldview-ui.js`): About Me home shows a **"People & relationships"** section with a "People in Your Life" row (count) → a dedicated **People editor** (`renderPeople`): per-person cards (name (relationship) — about, Private badge, Edit/Remove) and an add/edit form (name, relationship, about, "Private — never name this person" checkbox). Reuses `.wv-text` so the on-screen keyboard's existing scope + side-dock + `inputmode="none"` handling applies automatically. **Restart** now clears the graph too.
- **Relationship picker (v0.2.29):** the relationship field is a **standard grouped `<select>`** (`REL_GROUPS`: Family / Partner / Friends & social / Care & support / Pet — 30 options) **+ "Other…"** which reveals a free-text field. Case-insensitive match on edit re-selects a standard option or falls back to Other (prefilled). **One relationship per person for now** (single `me→person` edge type); multiple relationships with one person ("cousin" + "wife") is a deliberate later refinement (Ken, June 15 2026).
- **LLM wiring**: `llm.setRelationshipsBlock()` + inclusion in `buildProfileBlock()`; `app.js` `generateOptions()` calls it each turn alongside `setWorldviewBlock`. `app.js` init + `handleStart` load/sync the graph.
- CACHE_VERSION/APP_VERSION → `0.2.27` (build); → `0.2.28` (migration removed). `relationships.js` added to the sw precache. **Verified in preview:** model CRUD + private-withholding `buildBlock`; the People editor add→display→mark-private→badge flow; registry shows no A3 and `address_terms` under A1; after migration removal `migrateFromWorldview` is gone with CRUD/editor still working; no console errors. *On-disk persistence to `relationships.json` needs a granted FSA folder — confirm on the tablet.* **Scope held:** small typed people+edges with a free `attrs` bag — no graph DB, no third-party-relations editor yet.

**Decision (Ken, June 15 2026): relationship data is a separate concern in its own file — `relationships.json` — not flat question/answer fields.** Ken's insight: relationship data has a fundamentally different structure from the worldview questionnaire. It is a **graph** — *nodes* (people) and *edges* (relationships), **both of which carry their own data** — whereas the questionnaire is flat key→answer.

**Why the current model is wrong for this.** Today module **A3 "People in Your Life"** stores people as flat `repeat` lists (`household`, `family_key`, `pets` = arrays of `{name, relationship}` tuples; `friends_key` = a name list). That cannot represent: per-person attributes (how you address them, where they live, what you share, topics to avoid, how often you see them); the *same person* appearing in multiple lists with one identity; **edge** attributes (closeness, current state — "we're not talking right now" — shared history); or relationships *between third parties* ("my sister is married to my brother-in-law"). This matters specifically because the architecture's **relationship modeling** + Phase 2 **partner recognition** ask "the partner in front of me is *X* — who are they to me, what's our history?" — a node+edge lookup, not a questionnaire answer.

**Agreed shape (build-ready spec; coexists with the flat questionnaire):**
- New file in the FSA data folder: `relationships.json`, shaped `{ version, updated, people:[{id, name, attrs:{address_term, lives, likes, avoid, …}}], edges:[{from, to, type, attrs:{closeness, seen, notes, …}}] }`. `from:"me"` for the user↔person edges; person↔person edges allowed. `attrs` is a free-form bag per node and per edge (typed later as patterns emerge).
- New model layer `relationships.js` mirroring `worldview.js` conventions: data-folder write-through + localStorage cache, **and the same "file in the folder wins" reconciliation just settled in v0.2.25** (see the cross-PC fix above) — its own `syncToFolder`. Node/edge CRUD; privacy per node (contact-info-style strict phrase-around carries over); a `buildBlock()` contribution to the LLM profile (and, with partner recognition, a *focused* lookup for the current partner).
- The flat questionnaire **stays** for flat facts (name, place, interests, personality/values). The **A3 "People" module becomes the capture UI** that writes nodes/edges into the graph instead of `repeat` tuples; a migration reads existing A3 `repeat` answers into `people`/`edges`.

**Scope guardrail (Ken's "small, not a graph DB"):** build a small typed people+edges structure with a free `attrs` bag — *not* a full graph database or a third-party-relations editor — enough now, RAG-friendly later. Build is a dedicated session (model → migration → A3 UI rework → LLM wiring), authorized separately. Not started.

---

## My Places — situational awareness without GPS (Ken, August 3 2026), BUILT

Ken: *"Add support for 'My Places' to the Worldview model. For each place add 'facts' support. E.g., Place: Starbucks, Location: 123 main street (if specific to a particular location), 'favorite drink' = 'mocha latte', etc. Also need to add support for places in the Express panel just like the Person support that we already have. This is a way to support location situational awareness without GPS."* Built end-to-end and verified in preview.

**The framing that matters, and it is Ken's: a place is an INFLUENCER first and a stored record second.** Phase 2 was always going to get location from GPS. This gets the same signal by having the user *tap where they are*, exactly as they tap who they are talking with — which means it needs no new hardware permission, no Phase-2 dependency, and works indoors where GPS is worst. It also lands on a seam that already exists and is proven, so the whole feature is a patch, not a layer.

- **New model [`places.js`](app/js/places.js)** — `{ version, updated, places: [ { id, name, private, facts: [ { key, value } ] } ] }`, its own `places.json` + `aac_places` cache, the v0.2.25 file-in-folder-wins reconciliation. Mirrors `relationships.js` because it is the same kind of thing: user-owned data that is not questionnaire Q&A.
- **Facts are ARBITRARY named pairs, and there is deliberately NO fixed field set.** What is worth knowing about a coffee shop, a clinic and a cousin's house have almost nothing in common, so any schema we invented would be wrong for most places — which is why Ken's own example puts *Location* in as a fact with the qualifier "if specific to a particular location" rather than as a column. The editor offers 10 suggested fact names through a `datalist` (Location, What I usually order, Who I go with, …): quick to pick, equally quick to ignore.
- **Facts are an ORDERED ARRAY, not an object.** The user controls the order they are listed in (and so the order the model reads them), duplicate keys don't silently clobber, and a blank row can exist mid-typing. `normalizeFacts` drops rows with no key (an editor artifact) but **keeps a key with an empty value** — "Wi-Fi password: (blank)" is a fact someone has started recording, not junk.
- **Two blocks, and the split is load-bearing.** `buildBlock()` lists every place with its facts as standing context (`llm.setPlacesBlock`, alongside worldview + relationships). `buildHereBlock(id)` is the *situational* line for the place the user has tapped — it names the place and **repeats that place's facts**, so the current setting's details are in front of the model rather than one line among twenty. Where changes per conversation; what-you-know changes per edit.
- **Privacy is the standing three-level model, unchanged.** A private place is sent for context under the do-not-volunteer instruction, which names the two things that unlock it (partner asks / user steers via Reframe — see the privacy-model section above, whose circular-clause defect Ken found through this very sentence); `buildHereBlock` adds a matching do-not-name-it-on-your-own-initiative line. A place the user doesn't want the AI to know about is simply not added.
- **Express Panel `place` item type** — a toggle mirroring Partner exactly: picked from My Places by `placeId` in the editor (`— Choose a place —`), one active at a time, tap again to clear. **Olive `#827717`**, chosen because the palette had no yellow-green, so it cannot be confused with the nearest hues (pace deep-orange, feeling cyan). Tapping fires no round-trip — the effect lands at the next generation, so saying where you are never costs a token or interrupts a turn.
- **Turns are stamped with `place`** (`placeStamp()` → `{ id, label }`, keeping the stable `placeId`) alongside partner and feeling, so a reviewed conversation can join back to My Places — the Phase-3 relive-and-critique feature will want to know *where* an exchange happened.
- **The place PERSISTS across End conversation — `clearInfluencers()` does NOT clear it (Ken, August 3 2026).** A first cut cleared it alongside Partner and Feeling, by analogy with v0.5.31; Ken reversed it, and the reason is a genuine disanalogy rather than a preference. **Partner and Feeling are properties of the CONVERSATION** — it ends, and the next person and mood are genuinely unknown, so inheriting them is a wrong guess. **Where you are is a property of the ROOM, and ending a conversation does not move you.** A café visit or a clinic waiting room is several conversations in one place, so clearing would charge a re-tap for each. Cleared only by tapping the same place again or picking another. *(The risk this accepts, stated once: a place left on after the user has moved keeps shaping suggestions. It is visible — the button stays lit — and the frequency argument runs the other way, so the trade is the right one; the counter-argument is recorded rather than re-litigated.)*
- Also wired: sw precache, Export/Import (`places.json` joins the now-five user-owned files, with a count in the import summary), About Me **Restart** (clears places, and its confirmation text now says so), and load/sync at init, at Start, and on Choose-Folder.

**Verified in preview** (1280×800, stubbed fetch — no key needed): "My Places" on the About Me home; the form saves Ken's exact Starbucks example with both facts; **adding or removing a fact row preserves what is already typed** (the values are read back out of the DOM before every rebuild — the obvious way to write this loses them); edit prefills name + facts in order; the private badge and both privacy blocks are correct; the editor's `+ Place` and inline insert picker both work and the panel button renders olive with `aria-label="I'm at Starbucks"`; tapping it sets `aria-pressed`, tapping again clears, and it **survives End conversation** while Partner and Feeling drop; and a captured API request carries **both** the `Places I go` list and `The user is at Starbucks right now… What you know about Starbucks — Location: 123 Main Street; favorite drink: mocha latte`. 13 new unit tests in `tests/places.test.mjs`; 230/230 pass.

**Known limitation, existing behavior not a regression:** the Express Panel maps items 1:1 onto the layout's cells, so a place **appended** to a full default list (33 items, 33 cells) overflows and never renders. Use the row's **＋** insert button to place it within the grid. This has been true of every item type since v0.5.0; it is only more noticeable here because a new place is the first thing a user adds after the panel is already full.

## Conversation & Relationship Goals — three-layer model (design recorded June 15 2026; NOT built)

**Ken's insight (June 15 2026):** everyone has goals for every conversation — consciously or not — from "maintain the connection with this person" to "steer the conversation toward X." Goals should be a first-class input to generation. The key framing: **"relationship goals" is really *three distinct things on three time horizons*,** and they map to three different stores — lumping them together is what makes it hard.

**The three layers:**
1. **Disposition** — how you generally are with people (durable, trait-level): "I keep things light," "I want to be seen as capable." Not tied to one person or conversation; the *prior/default stance*.
2. **Standing relationship goal** — what you want from a *specific relationship* (durable, person-level): "stay close to my sister," "I want my boss to see me as competent." Stable across many conversations with that person.
3. **Conversation goal** — what you want from *this exchange right now* (ephemeral, session-level): "today I need to ask Dad for help," "steer toward planning the trip." Set at/just-before conversation start, injected into that conversation's prompt, then discarded.

**Mapping to existing stores (two of three already have a home):**
| Layer | Store | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| Disposition | `worldview.json` **Tier-B** (how you treat different people, register/values) | Exactly what Tier-B is for; designed, not yet built. |
| Standing relationship goal | `relationships.json` **edge `attrs`** (e.g. `attrs.goals`) | **Clean fit today, no schema change** — a goal is an attribute of the `me→person` edge. |
| Conversation goal | **Session runtime** (Conversation Engine state), optionally appended to the `conversations/*.json` log for Phase-3 review | Genuinely new, but it's runtime state, **not a new persistent store**. |

**Recording mechanism (per layer):**
- Standing relationship goal → a **"what I want from this relationship"** control in the **People editor** (the one built in v0.2.27), writing to the edge `attrs`.
- Conversation goal → set at **conversation initiation** (pairs with Phase-1 canned starters and Phase-2 partner recognition — "you're talking to Dad; what do you want from this?"), with a quick mid-conversation control (fits the persistent-controls region); stored in session memory.
- Disposition → Tier-B questionnaire when built.
- **Expression format — DECIDED (Ken, June 15 2026): curated menu + free text.** A short list of common goals (stay close / be understood / be seen as capable / keep it light / get support / repair things) **plus** an "in my own words" option — fast to pick (matters for the CP target user) yet still personal. (Not menu-only, not free-text-only.)

**Why it matters / how it's used:** goals shape and **rank the four-slot move palette** (PREFERRED / DISPREFERRED / INITIATIVE / REPAIR): "maintain connection" → favor affiliative PREFERRED; "steer toward X" → surface a high-ranked INITIATIVE toward X; "wind down" → favor PRE-CLOSING. They become explicit generation inputs, **layered most-specific-wins** exactly like the prompt blocks already are: disposition (worldview) → standing relationship goal (graph) → conversation goal (session).

**Nice property:** goals are **never spoken** — they only steer generation — so they sidestep the privacy machinery entirely (unlike facts, a goal can't be accidentally said aloud).

**Open sub-questions (not yet decided — pin before building, so they aren't silently assumed):**
1. **Single vs. multiple goals per layer.** The mapping sketches `attrs.goals` (plural), but we only decided single-*relationship* (the relationship picker). Whether a relationship or a conversation can hold more than one goal at once is undecided. (Parallels the single-relationship-per-person call.)
2. **User-set vs. system-suggested goals.** Everything here assumes the user sets the goal explicitly. Whether the system may ever *infer/suggest* a goal (from partner recognition, history, etc.) is open — current default is explicit/user-set only.
3. **Per-person vs. relationship-type goals.** "With authority figures I want to seem competent" is a goal on a relationship *type*, not one person. The graph stores `type` on the edge, not as its own node, so type-level goals would need a convention (or a small types table). For now goals would attach per-person only.

**Status:** design recorded; expression format decided (curated menu + free text); three sub-questions above left open. **Build deferred** (Ken: "just record the design") — revisit when the conversation-engine work matures. Standing relationship goals are the smallest first build when we resume (they slot straight onto the existing graph edge + People editor).

**Related reference doc:** `Reframe-Concepts-for-AAC-Architecture.docx` (July 13 2026) — the academic grounding for *redirect* generally (Goffman's footing, SFL register theory, Appraisal Theory, pragmatics/politeness). Its curated-menu-plus-free-text reasoning (Section 5) is the same reasoning already applied to the "Expression format" decision above; its 7-category redirect taxonomy is a candidate source list for the conversation-goal menu's options, alongside the "stay close / be understood / …" set already chosen. **Section 7 of that document (added July 13 2026) grounds this section directly:** Dillard's Goals-Plans-Action model (primary vs. secondary goals) and Canary & Stafford's Relational Maintenance Theory, mapped explicitly onto this three-layer model — see the concrete organizational structure below, which builds on that grounding.

**Concrete organizational structure — proposed July 13 2026 (Ken + Claude; not yet built).** A specific plan for what the curated menus contain and where the controls live, worked out in conversation and building on the Dillard/Canary & Stafford grounding above.

- **Shared vocabulary — three curated lists, no free text as the primary input.** Constraint (Ken): the target user can only speak in general/generic terms about a goal, whether the partner is a stranger or someone well known.
  - **List A — primary conversation goal** (Dillard's primary-goal categories; generic across any partner, known or arbitrary): Stay connected / catch up · Get information or advice · Ask for help · Share news or feelings · Make plans together · Repair something · Ask for something specific · Just be sociable, no agenda.
  - **List B — stance modifier** (reuses the Reframe redirect taxonomy verbatim — one vocabulary serving both a standing goal and a live mid-conversation Reframe): Come across as capable · Keep it warm · Keep it brief · Stay calm / avoid conflict · Keep it smooth and on-topic.
  - **List C — relationship-maintenance goal** (Canary & Stafford's five strategies; only offered for graph people, since "maintain" presupposes an existing relationship): Be upbeat with them · Talk openly about the relationship · Reassure them of your commitment · Do things together · Support their other relationships.

- **WHERE EACH LIST IS SOURCED FROM — person and place populate DIFFERENT lists, so they compose rather than compete (Ken + Claude, August 5 2026).** Ken's question was whether goals revolve primarily around the partner or the place. **Standing relationship goals are 100% person by definition** — you have no relationship with a coffee shop, so nothing can hang a standing goal off a place. Per-conversation goals split by whether the partner is present as an **identity** or as a **role**: talking to your sister, the goal comes from her and the setting is incidental (same goal at her house or a café); at a pharmacy, clinic, or counter the partner is often a stranger you will never see again, and the goal comes from what the place is *for*. **The place determines the goal precisely when it determines the partner's ROLE rather than their IDENTITY** — which is the project's own founding distinction, transactional vs. interactional communication, so the two goal sources correspond to the two modes the product exists to span.
  - **The resolution is not a priority rule.** **Place → List A** (the primary goal: what this exchange is *for*), strongest in transactional settings. **Person → Lists B and C** (stance and maintenance: how to *be* with them), and List A as well when the conversation is relational. Test case that breaks a naive "person outranks place": the pharmacist you happen to know — Ramon supplies the stance (keep it friendly), the pharmacy supplies the primary goal (pick up the prescription), and they compose. *(A "person outranks place" precedence rule was the obvious first answer and is wrong for exactly this case.)*
  - **A place can already carry a goal with NO schema change** — `places.js` facts are arbitrary named pairs, so a `Goal` fact is expressible today, the same way `Location` is just a fact.
  - **The third source is the one that cannot be inferred and therefore most needs the UI: the user's own agenda** ("today I need to tell someone what happened"). Neither the person nor the place implies it.
  - **This converges with the context-scoped Express Panel work** (see the context-sensitive page-sets entry): a place- or person-scoped framing chip **is** a one-tap conversation goal for that context. So the Start-flow Goal picker may end up needed only for the third source, with the common cases pre-filled or reachable from the panel.

- **Arbitrary vs. graph people.** The per-conversation Goal control (List A, optionally + List B) is identical whether the partner is a stranger or someone in `relationships.json` — it never depends on the graph. The only difference: a graph person with a standing goal recorded (List A ∪ List C, via the People editor) pre-highlights that goal as the suggested default when they're the active Partner — one tap to accept, or override for this conversation. An arbitrary/unrecognized partner gets the same blank menu with no pre-suggestion. List C never appears in the live per-conversation control — it is a People-editor-only, standing attribute.

- **UI placement — supersedes the "quick mid-conversation control… fits the persistent-controls region" idea above with a concrete design:**
  1. **Primary: a Goal-picker page folded into the Start-conversation flow.** Reuses the reserved response-footprint cards that already show openers (`handleInitiate` → INITIATING mode) — no new region, no reflow (UI Layout Rule 1). Tapping "Start conversation" shows Goal chips (List A + a "Decide as we go" chip) in that footprint first; tapping one advances to the existing opener-card page in the same footprint. Net cost for the common case: one extra tap ahead of the existing "pick an opener" tap.
  2. **Revise: a small tappable "Goal" chip in the transcript header**, alongside the mode chip and the Start/End conversation icons (v0.5.40) — shows the current goal's short label (or "No goal set"), informational-but-tappable like the mode chip. Tapping it re-shows the same Goal-picker page as a brief overlay on the response footprint (Rule 8's modal-superimposition mechanism), then returns to whatever palette was showing.
  3. **Deliberately NOT an Express Panel toggle**, unlike Partner and Feeling. Rationale: importance and churn rate are different axes — Goal is high-importance (Dillard's *primary* goal, the reason the exchange exists) but low-churn (typically set once per conversation, like Partner's own use pattern), whereas Feeling is lower-stakes but plausibly changes moment-to-moment within a conversation. The Express Panel's scarce, high-frequency real estate stays reserved for Feeling; Goal gets a placement matched to its own, lower churn instead.

- **Resolves open sub-question 1 above as a v1 default:** single active goal per layer (not multiple) — consistent with the existing single-relationship-per-person call. Sub-questions 2 (user-set vs. system-suggested) and 3 (per-person vs. relationship-type) remain open.

- **What's unchanged:** Feeling stays exactly as shipped (Express Panel toggle); the storage-mapping table above, the layered most-specific-wins resolution, and `buildSituationBlock` injection + per-turn stamping (mirroring the existing Partner/Feeling stamp pattern, v0.5.30) all carry over unchanged.
